    Contents

      0. Build dependency configuration

      Pass optional vector dependencies through CMake options rather than hard-coding local paths.

      Currently supported source-configuration options:

      Formats Description Default
      -DHAVE_VECTOR_INDEX={ON|OFF} Enables or disables the vector feature at build time. Pass ON for vector builds and OFF for an upstream-compatible behavior profile. OFF
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS={ON|OFF} Enables the Faiss backend. When set to OFF, all Faiss path options are ignored and indexes cannot be created with the FAISS provider. ON
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_HNSW={ON|OFF} Enables the hnswlib backend. When set to OFF, all hnswlib path options are ignored and indexes cannot be created with the HNSWLIB provider. ON
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN={ON|OFF} Enables the DiskANN backend. When set to OFF, all DiskANN path options are ignored and indexes cannot be created with the DISKANN provider. ON
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_ROOT=path Faiss source or install root. A source tree with CMakeLists.txt is built as part of MySQL. Empty
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_INCLUDES=path Faiss header directory for a prebuilt Faiss installation. Empty
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_LIB_DIR=path Directory that contains prebuilt Faiss libraries. Empty
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_STATIC={ON|OFF} When ON, only static Faiss libraries are searched. The default searches platform shared libraries. OFF
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_HNSWLIB_ROOT=path hnswlib source root or include root. Empty
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_HNSWLIB_INCLUDES=path hnswlib header directory when no source root is provided. Empty
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_ROOT=path Official DiskANN source tree. CMake detects a cpp_main C++ tree or a Rust/Garnet workspace and builds the offline adapter or Garnet serial fallback accordingly. Empty
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_INCLUDES=path cpp_main header directory, such as the directory containing pq_flash_index.h, when using a prebuilt official DiskANN C++ libdiskann. Empty
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_LIB_DIR=path Directory that contains the official DiskANN C++ libdiskann or Rust/Garnet libdiskann_garnet. Prebuilt MySQL offline adapter libraries are not accepted as a user-facing mode. Empty
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_STATIC={ON|OFF} When ON, only static libdiskann.a is searched. The default searches platform shared libraries. OFF
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_ENABLE_NATIVE_SIMD={ON|OFF} Enables native SIMD flags for supported vector backend compilation units. ON
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_OPT_LEVEL={generic|dd|avx2|avx512|avx512_spr|sve} Selects the Faiss SIMD target, such as generic, avx2, avx512, avx512_spr, sve, or runtime-dispatch dd. Empty; selects dd on Linux when MYSQL_VECTOR_ENABLE_NATIVE_SIMD=ON, otherwise generic.

      Build constraints and option precedence

      This table lists invalid option combinations, required companion options, and configuration precedence.

      Option Combination Constraint Type Result Recommendation
      -DHAVE_VECTOR_INDEX=OFF with any -DMYSQL_VECTOR_* backend option No effect The server is built with standard no-vector MySQL behavior. Vector backend options do not enable vector functions, status variables, or index support. Pass -DHAVE_VECTOR_INDEX=ON for vector builds. For no-vector compatibility builds, do not pass backend path options.
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS=OFF with any MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_ROOT, MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_INCLUDES, MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_LIB_DIR, or MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_STATIC No effect Faiss paths and static-library selection are ignored. The current binary cannot create indexes with the FAISS provider. Set -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS=ON when Faiss is required. Otherwise, omit Faiss path options.
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_HNSW=OFF with any MYSQL_VECTOR_HNSWLIB_ROOT or MYSQL_VECTOR_HNSWLIB_INCLUDES No effect hnswlib paths are ignored. The current binary cannot create indexes with the HNSWLIB provider. Set -DMYSQL_VECTOR_HNSW=ON when HNSWLIB is required. Otherwise, omit hnswlib paths.
      -DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN=OFF with any MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_ROOT, MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_INCLUDES, MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_LIB_DIR, or MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_STATIC No effect DiskANN paths and static-library selection are ignored. The current binary cannot create indexes with the DISKANN provider. Set -DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN=ON when DiskANN is required. Otherwise, omit DiskANN paths.
      MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_ROOT points to a Faiss source tree while MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_LIB_DIR is also passed Precedence The source tree wins. CMake builds the Faiss target from source, and the prebuilt library directory is not used as the direct Faiss backend input. Choose either source build or prebuilt library mode. To use a prebuilt library, do not point MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_ROOT at a source tree.
      MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_ROOT points to a Rust/Garnet workspace while MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_STATIC=ON is passed No effect The Rust/Garnet source build produces a platform shared library. MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_STATIC only affects prebuilt library lookup through MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_LIB_DIR. Use MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_STATIC=ON only when MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_LIB_DIR points to a prebuilt libdiskann.a.
      Legacy options such as MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_INCLUDE_DIR, MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_LIBRARY_DIR, and MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_LIB Invalid CMake fails. Legacy options were removed to avoid multiple names for the same meaning. Use the current *_INCLUDES, *_LIB_DIR, *_STATIC, and *_ROOT options from the build option table.

      Notes:

      • Vector builds must pass -DHAVE_VECTOR_INDEX=ON explicitly. If -DHAVE_VECTOR_INDEX=OFF is passed, or the option is not enabled, the server is built with standard no-vector MySQL behavior: vector functions, vector status variables, the vector transaction plugin, and vector DD truth-store are not registered; only the command skeleton required by the parser remains; the server does not register the Com_load_vector status variable or its Performance Schema statement class; VECTOR(dim), CREATE/DROP VECTOR INDEX, and SHOW VECTOR STATUS return unsupported errors.
      • MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS, MYSQL_VECTOR_HNSW, and MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN are library-level master switches. When a library is set to OFF, CMake ignores that library's ROOT, INCLUDES, LIB_DIR, and STATIC options. Inspect the current binary through INFORMATION_SCHEMA.VECTOR_LIBRARIES.
      • If a library is not compiled into the current binary, creating an index with that provider fails. For example, MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN=OFF prevents indexes using provider='DISKANN'.
      • Faiss has two build modes:
      • If MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_ROOT points to a Faiss source tree with CMakeLists.txt, MySQL builds the CPU Faiss library as part of the MySQL build.
      • Otherwise CMake falls back to the traditional mode and looks for existing Faiss headers and libraries.
      • When Faiss is built from source on Linux and MYSQL_VECTOR_ENABLE_NATIVE_SIMD=ON, the default is equivalent to MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_OPT_LEVEL=dd, using Faiss runtime SIMD dispatch in the main library; pass -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_OPT_LEVEL=generic explicitly when a portable binary is required.
      • hnswlib is integrated as a header-only backend. MYSQL_VECTOR_HNSWLIB_ROOT can point directly to the source root; CMake adds it as a subdirectory and does not require a prebuilt standalone library.
      • When MYSQL_VECTOR_ENABLE_NATIVE_SIMD=ON, CMake applies the supported -march=native or -mcpu=native flag only to sql/vector/vector_index_backend_hnswlib.cc. This lets hnswlib distance kernels instantiate SIMD code for the current CPU. To build a portable binary for different CPU models, pass -DMYSQL_VECTOR_ENABLE_NATIVE_SIMD=OFF.
      • Batch search uses vector_batch_search_threads=32 by default. HNSWLIB, Faiss, and DiskANN can override it with vector_hnsw_search_threads, vector_faiss_search_threads, and vector_diskann_search_threads. The effective worker count is clamped to the smaller of the query count and configured worker count. vector_search_batch_count and vector_search_batch_result_count control the accepted query count and total result slots for each batch call.
      • DiskANN offline index loading is controlled by vector_diskann_offline_search_threads, vector_diskann_search_io_limit, and vector_diskann_cache_nodes. vector_diskann_search_complexity sets the default query-time search list size for new indexes, and VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_COMPLEXITY() can override it for one existing index.
      • vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_size controls the total PQ code budget for DiskANN offline build. 0 derives the budget from vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_ratio. The index-level VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_PQ_CODE_BUDGET_SIZE() setting takes precedence over the global variable. It is build-time configuration; changing it on an existing index requires an explicit rebuild before the serving index uses the new value, and setting the same value again is a no-op that does not unload an already loaded DiskANN runtime.
      • If Faiss or another vector dependency resolves BLAS symbols through OpenBLAS or OpenMP on the current system, and tests show internal worker threads interfering with mysqld signal handling or benchmark repeatability, limit only the startup environment of the tested mysqld or standalone benchmark instance. Do not inject these vector-test environment variables into mysqld_safe, systemd templates, or other upstream startup scripts; standard MySQL builds and no-vector builds must keep upstream startup behavior. In MTR, do not export these variables in the mysql-test-run.pl process environment, because that can affect MTR's NUMBER_OF_CPUS detection and incorrectly skip resource group tests. Example:
      cmake --build "${BUILD_DEBUG_DIR}" --target mysqld

cd "${BUILD_DEBUG_DIR}/mysql-test"
./mysql-test-run.pl --suite=vector \
  --mysqld-env=OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1 \
  --mysqld-env=OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 \
  --mysqld-env=GOTO_NUM_THREADS=1

      If mysqld or a benchmark client is started directly outside the test framework, set these variables in that process environment rather than relying on MySQL startup scripts to inject them.

      • hnswlib bulk build / rebuild uses the hardware thread count by default and clamps the actual worker count to the workload size, hardware thread count, and 65535. The global system variable vector_hnsw_build_threads can specify the default parallelism for new HNSWLIB indexes, and VEC_INDEX_CREATE(..., build_threads) / CREATE VECTOR INDEX ... WITH (..., build_threads) can override it per index. The value is stored in HNSWLIB index metadata as hnsw_build_threads. A statement-level value greater than 0 takes precedence over the global variable; statement-level 0 means inherit the global setting, and only a global 0 selects the automatic strategy.
      • Faiss build / rebuild relies on Faiss's OpenMP parallel path. The global system variable vector_faiss_build_threads can specify the default OpenMP worker count for new FAISS indexes, and VEC_INDEX_CREATE(..., build_threads) / CREATE VECTOR INDEX ... WITH (..., build_threads) can override it per index. A statement-level value greater than 0 takes precedence over the global variable; statement-level 0 means inherit the global setting, and a global 0 lets Faiss/OpenMP choose automatically. The range is 0..65535.
      • DiskANN uses the official source tree passed through MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_ROOT to select the build strategy automatically: a cpp_main C++ tree builds the MySQL DiskANN offline adapter, while a Rust/Garnet workspace is built with cargo as the Garnet serial fallback. Only when source build is unavailable should MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_LIB_DIR be used with an official C++ libdiskann plus matching cpp_main headers, or with a Rust/Garnet libdiskann_garnet. User-provided prebuilt MySQL offline adapter libraries are not a supported public mode. If MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_ROOT points to a Rust/Garnet workspace while MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_LIB_DIR points to the official C++ libdiskann and MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_INCLUDES points to matching headers, the same MySQL build can enable both the Garnet serial fallback and the DiskANN offline adapter. If only the Garnet serial ABI with create_index and insert is available at runtime, MySQL-Vector calls insert row by row to complete shadow build. vector_diskann_build_threads and statement-level build_threads are recorded in index configuration but do not change official serial-ABI parallelism.
      • DiskANN build mode can force or relax that runtime choice: auto prefers the MySQL-Vector vendored runtime/offline adapter and falls back to the official serial ABI when offline is unavailable or the input is too small for the minimum graph; other offline build failures do not automatically downgrade. serial forces the official serial ABI; offline uses only the vendored runtime/offline adapter. Explicit offline rebuild fails when the adapter is not configured or cannot be loaded; it does not fall back to serial insertion. This value has no operational effect for non-DiskANN indexes; they report auto only as the default status value.
      • vector_diskann_build_mode sets the default DiskANN build mode for indexes that do not have an explicit index-level mode. Accepted values are auto, serial, and offline. The default is auto.
      • vector_diskann_pq_runtime controls the DiskANN PQ/kmeans runtime. The default native_auto generates MySQL-Vector native PQ artifacts and passes them through the native PQ graph/cache bridge for the DiskANN load/search file layout; official keeps the upstream DiskANN PQ/kmeans path for debugging or comparison. native_auto uses deterministic training samples and block streaming encoding under vector_diskann_build_memory_size; when the budget is tight, it first reduces PQ workers, then shrinks encode block rows, and only falls back to the official PQ/kmeans path if the bounded working set still cannot fit. native_strict uses the same Native Runtime but disables fallback: a memory-budget, artifact-generation, graph/cache bridge, file-layout/checksum, ordinal/doc-id mapping, load, or search-result-shape failure aborts the build and removes incomplete artifacts. DiskANN search is approximate, so a fixed validation sample that is absent from its own topK is retained as a quality diagnostic; that miss alone neither fails native_strict nor triggers native_auto fallback. Use native_strict when a performance test must prove that the Native Runtime handled the complete build.
      • vector_diskann_build_threads can specify the default parallelism for new DiskANN indexes, and VEC_INDEX_CREATE(..., build_threads) / CREATE VECTOR INDEX ... WITH (..., build_threads) can override it per index. A statement-level value greater than 0 takes precedence over the global variable; statement-level 0 means inherit the global setting, and a global 0 lets the DiskANN runtime choose automatically. Explicit values use the range 0..65535. With an official serial-only DiskANN shared library, this setting only preserves configuration semantics and does not create parallelism.
      • vector_diskann_build_blas_threads controls the OpenMP/OpenBLAS/MKL worker count while the DiskANN offline adapter calls the official offline build path. The default 1 avoids multiplying outer OpenMP build workers by inner PQ or numeric-library workers in the official DiskANN offline build path. 0 leaves inner PQ and numeric-library thread selection unchanged. This is a runtime resource-control option, is not written to index metadata, and does not affect the official serial ABI.
      • vector_diskann_max_degree and vector_diskann_build_complexity set the default build parameters for new DiskANN indexes. CREATE VECTOR INDEX captures these values before backfill, so the first build uses the target graph parameters directly. Existing indexes still use VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_BUILD_PARAMS() followed by an explicit rebuild when their parameters need to change.
      • The DiskANN source-build path is used for the internal MySQL-Vector vendored runtime and offline adapter. Garnet serial fallback is still loaded through dlopen(), so when that fallback is used, pass either the full shared-library path or at least the shared-library directory. The official cpp_main CMake build selects gcc/g++; MySQL-Vector lets that child build detect matching OpenMP settings independently, while the MySQL parent build may still use another compiler such as Clang.
      • When using prebuilt DiskANN libraries, MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_LIB_DIR must point to the directory that actually contains libdiskann or libdiskann_garnet, not an arbitrary parent directory. If the directory contains the C++ libdiskann, also pass matching cpp_main headers through MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_INCLUDES.
      • CMake also reads the same-named environment variables MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS, MYSQL_VECTOR_HNSW, MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN, MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_ROOT, MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_INCLUDES, MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_LIB_DIR, MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_STATIC, MYSQL_VECTOR_HNSWLIB_ROOT, MYSQL_VECTOR_HNSWLIB_INCLUDES, MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_ROOT, MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_INCLUDES, MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_LIB_DIR, MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_STATIC as defaults for temporary local builds. Reproducible builds and CI should prefer explicit CMake options.

      Example:

      BUILD_ROOT=../build/mysql-vector
BUILD_DEBUG_DIR="${BUILD_ROOT}/build-debug"
BOOST_ROOT="${BUILD_ROOT}/deps"
FAISS_ROOT=../deps/FAISS
HNSWLIB_ROOT=../deps/HNSWLib
DISKANN_ROOT=../deps/DiskANN

cmake -S . -B "${BUILD_DEBUG_DIR}" -G Ninja \
-DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug \
-DWITH_DEBUG=1 \
-DHAVE_VECTOR_INDEX=ON \
-DWITH_NDB=OFF \
-DWITH_NDBCLUSTER_STORAGE_ENGINE=OFF \
-DDOWNLOAD_BOOST=1 \
-DWITH_BOOST="${BOOST_ROOT}" \
-DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_ROOT="${FAISS_ROOT}" \
-DMYSQL_VECTOR_HNSWLIB_ROOT="${HNSWLIB_ROOT}" \
-DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_ROOT="${DISKANN_ROOT}"

      This branch uses unmodified official DiskANN. MySQL-Vector first uses its internal vendored DiskANN runtime for structured build, load, search, and batch search. That runtime is a MySQL-Vector-owned execution layer, is not a user-visible provider. If the runtime is unavailable, the path falls back through the official_cpp_main offline adapter and then the Garnet serial fallback.

      When MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_ROOT points to a cpp_main C++ tree, CMake builds the MySQL DiskANN offline adapter; when it points to a Rust/Garnet workspace, CMake uses cargo to build the libdiskann_garnet serial fallback. The default auto mode uses the offline adapter first when it can load; otherwise it falls back to Garnet serial insert. Explicit offline rebuild fails instead of falling back to serial insertion.

      License/NOTICE boundary: the vendored DiskANN runtime lives under extra/vector/diskann-vendored/ and contains only MySQL-Vector-owned wrapper and runtime code. DiskANN, FAISS, and HNSWLib are still integrated as third-party dependencies under their upstream licenses.

      MySQL Vector does not support user-provided prebuilt libmysql_vector_diskann_offline_adapter as a public integration mode. To use the DiskANN offline path, provide the official C++ libdiskann and matching cpp_main headers, and let MySQL Vector build the matching internal adapter from extra/vector/diskann-offline-adapter-cpp.

      Example for building the official DiskANN C++ libdiskann:

      git clone https://github.com/microsoft/DiskANN.git
cd DiskANN
git checkout 78256bbab4685e1774e78d331e081a153be26823
cmake -S . -B build-release -G Ninja \
  -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release \
  -DBUILD_SHARED_LIBS=ON \
  -DCMAKE_POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE=ON
cmake --build build-release --parallel

# Linux usually produces build-release/src/libdiskann.so.
# macOS usually produces build-release/src/libdiskann.dylib.
# When using a prebuilt libdiskann, pass both the library directory and
# the cpp_main header directory.

      If a prebuilt libdiskann is downloaded from an OS package, internal artifact repository, or another trusted source, verify that it was built from a compatible official cpp_main tree and provide the matching headers. MySQL Vector cannot build the offline adapter from the library file alone.

      If a static libdiskann.a is required, the official C++ CMake build does not need source edits. Pass BUILD_SHARED_LIBS=OFF. This command was verified on Ubuntu 24.04 to produce build-static/src/libdiskann.a, and the MySQL Vector offline adapter can link that static library.

      git clone https://github.com/microsoft/DiskANN.git
cd DiskANN
git checkout 78256bbab4685e1774e78d331e081a153be26823
cmake -S . -B build-static -G Ninja \
  -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release \
  -DBUILD_SHARED_LIBS=OFF \
  -DCMAKE_POSITION_INDEPENDENT_CODE=ON
cmake --build build-static --target diskann --parallel

# Output: build-static/src/libdiskann.a
# MySQL Vector still builds a shared offline adapter when libdiskann is static;
# the adapter no longer needs to load libdiskann.so at runtime.

      Example for building the DiskANN Rust/Garnet serial fallback libdiskann_garnet:

      git clone https://github.com/microsoft/DiskANN.git
cd DiskANN
git checkout fabcb9b1d6a4f6a5c2714e1fa44ea9ebe0ee8d1f
cargo build --release -p diskann-garnet

# Linux produces target/release/libdiskann_garnet.so.
# macOS produces target/release/libdiskann_garnet.dylib.
# The current official Garnet crate is a cdylib by default and does not
# produce a .a static library by default.

      The current MySQL-Vector Garnet adapter is ABI v2. It supports only the latest Garnet 5.0.0 public FFI pinned in the version table and does not retain runtime compatibility branches for earlier callback or search argument layouts.

      The current DiskANN Rust/Garnet workspace uses resolver = "3". Ubuntu 24.04's official cargo 1.75 cannot parse that workspace. Use rustup to install a newer stable Rust toolchain before building libdiskann_garnet from source.

      sudo apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends rustup
rustup default stable
cargo build --release -p diskann-garnet

      This branch uses unmodified upstream FAISS, HNSWLib, and DiskANN. The MySQL-Vector native runtime invokes these libraries for standalone indexes. The high-performance DiskANN path prefers the internal vendored runtime; it does not depend on an external vector execution engine or require changes to upstream DiskANN.

      0.1 Ubuntu packages and source dependencies

      These Ubuntu 24.04 LTS results come from apt-cache policy, apt-cache search, and minimal compile smoke checks.

      Dependency Ubuntu package Install or build path Verified result
      hnswlib libhnswlib-dev sudo apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends libhnswlib-dev Verified version 0.8.0-1build1. Headers are under /usr/include/hnswlib/ and compile with g++ -std=c++17.
      FAISS No candidate for libfaiss-dev, python3-faiss, or faiss Build from official source, or use a user-maintained prebuilt library and pass MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_ROOT / MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_LIB_DIR. No official FAISS development package was found in the enabled Ubuntu 24.04 repositories.
      DiskANN C++ No candidate for diskann or libdiskann-dev Build libdiskann.so, libdiskann.dylib, or libdiskann.a from the official cpp_main source. Verified that BUILD_SHARED_LIBS=OFF produces libdiskann.a and that the MySQL Vector offline adapter links against it.
      DiskANN Rust/Garnet No standalone DiskANN Garnet development package was found Build libdiskann_garnet.so / .dylib from the official Rust workspace with a recent Rust toolchain. Verified that Ubuntu 24.04's official cargo 1.75 cannot parse the current workspace, while rustup stable can build libdiskann_garnet.so.

      HNSWLib is header-only. If a source tree is not used, Ubuntu 24.04 can install the packaged headers directly:

      sudo apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends libhnswlib-dev

      FAISS has no available Ubuntu 24.04 development package in the enabled repositories. When building CPU Faiss from source, install the BLAS/LAPACK/OpenMP dependencies first. OpenBLAS is the default recommendation:

      sudo apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
  build-essential cmake ninja-build \
  libopenblas-dev liblapack-dev libomp-dev

      DiskANN C++ build dependencies can be installed from Ubuntu packages. libmkl-dev is in Ubuntu multiverse; enable that component first if it is not already enabled.

      sudo add-apt-repository multiverse
sudo apt-get update
sudo apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
  build-essential cmake ninja-build \
  libboost-program-options-dev libaio-dev libgoogle-perftools-dev \
  libomp-dev libmkl-dev

      If Faiss should not be built during the MySQL build, explicitly point to an existing library installation:

      FAISS_INSTALL_ROOT=../deps/faiss-install
FAISS_LIB_DIR="${FAISS_INSTALL_ROOT}/lib"

-DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_INCLUDES="${FAISS_INSTALL_ROOT}/include" \
-DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_LIB_DIR="${FAISS_LIB_DIR}"

      0.1 Runtime system variables

      Command-Line Format System Variable Scope Dynamic Type Default Minimum Value Maximum Value / Valid Values Unit Description
      --vector-default-library=name vector_default_library Global Yes Enumeration First compiled library in diskann, hnsw, faiss order; empty when none is compiled '', diskann, hnsw, faiss Default library for provider-omitted vector index creation. Setting an uncompiled library raises an error; empty means no default library.
      --vector-hnsw-build-threads=# vector_hnsw_build_threads Global Yes Integer 0 0 65535 Default worker count for new HNSWLIB build / rebuild operations. 0 means automatic.
      --vector-faiss-build-threads=# vector_faiss_build_threads Global Yes Integer 0 0 65535 Default Faiss/OpenMP worker count for new FAISS build / rebuild operations. 0 lets Faiss choose.
      --vector-diskann-build-threads=# vector_diskann_build_threads Global Yes Integer 0 0 65535 Default worker count for new DiskANN build / rebuild operations. 0 lets the DiskANN runtime choose.
      --vector-diskann-build-blas-threads=# vector_diskann_build_blas_threads Global Yes Integer 1 0 65535 OpenMP/OpenBLAS/MKL worker count inside each DiskANN offline PQ chunk worker. The default 1 avoids multiplying outer build workers by inner PQ or numeric-library workers; 0 leaves inner PQ and numeric-library thread selection unchanged.
      --vector-build-pipeline-mode={auto|direct|segmented} vector_build_pipeline_mode Global Yes Enumeration auto auto, direct, segmented Global build pipeline selector. auto chooses direct or segmented by input size, direct forces the short path, and segmented forces the segment task path.
      --vector-diskann-segment-profile={manual|throughput|balanced|high_recall} vector_diskann_segment_profile Global Yes Enumeration manual manual, throughput, balanced, high_recall DiskANN standalone offline raw segment profile. manual uses the explicit segment variables, throughput favors larger segments, balanced uses about a 512MiB target, and high_recall uses about a 256MiB target for high-dimensional data to improve recall.
      --vector-build-pipeline-min-rows=# vector_build_pipeline_min_rows Global Yes Integer 1048576 0 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) rows Row-count threshold that makes auto choose the segmented pipeline. 0 disables the row-count trigger.
      --vector-build-pipeline-min-size=# vector_build_pipeline_min_size Global Yes Integer 4294967296 0 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) bytes Vector-payload threshold that makes auto choose the segmented pipeline. Configuration files accept K/M/G suffixes, while online SET uses bytes. 0 disables the size trigger.
      --vector-build-segment-max-rows=# vector_build_segment_max_rows Global Yes Integer 4294967295 1 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) rows Safety row cap for one raw segment in the segmented pipeline. The default is close to the maximum row count representable by the FBIN row header, so vector_build_segment_target_size controls segment sizing first. If a smaller value is set explicitly, LOAD VECTOR DATA rolls to the next raw segment when that row cap is reached.
      --vector-build-segment-target-size=# vector_build_segment_target_size Global Yes Integer 2147483648 1048576 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) bytes Target vector payload limit for one raw segment in the segmented pipeline. The default is 2GiB to balance build invocations, query fan-out, and recall tuning headroom. Configuration files accept K/M/G suffixes, while online SET uses bytes.
      --vector-build-pipeline-max-tasks=# vector_build_pipeline_max_tasks Global Yes Integer 1 1 65535 tasks Maximum concurrent segment build tasks for one index rebuild. Large DiskANN builds default to 1 to avoid multiple build tasks competing for memory and CPU.
      --vector-diskann-segmented-serving[={OFF|ON}] vector_diskann_segmented_serving Global Yes Boolean ON OFF, ON Controls the serving shape for DiskANN raw-only standalone/offline builds with multiple raw segments. The default ON builds one backend per raw segment and fans out/merges search results. OFF merges raw segments into one logical DiskANN offline index and is intended only as a diagnostic fallback.
      --vector-build-pipeline-progress-interval=# vector_build_pipeline_progress_interval Global Yes Integer 10 1 3600 seconds Minimum interval for build task progress and status field updates.
      --vector-search-batch-count=# vector_search_batch_count Global Yes Integer 1024 1 65535 Maximum query vector count accepted by a single VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_BATCH() call.
      --vector-search-batch-result-count=# vector_search_batch_result_count Global Yes Integer 65536 1 1048576 Maximum query_count * top_k result slots accepted by a single VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_BATCH() call.
      --vector-batch-search-threads=# vector_batch_search_threads Global Yes Integer 32 0 65535 Default worker count for batch search across all backends. 0 uses hardware-thread based sizing, and execution clamps the effective value to the query count and configured worker count.
      --vector-hnsw-search-threads=# vector_hnsw_search_threads Global Yes Integer 0 0 65535 HNSWLIB batch search worker override. 0 inherits vector_batch_search_threads.
      --vector-faiss-search-threads=# vector_faiss_search_threads Global Yes Integer 0 0 65535 FAISS batch search worker override. 0 inherits vector_batch_search_threads.
      --vector-diskann-search-threads=# vector_diskann_search_threads Global Yes Integer 0 0 65535 DiskANN batch search worker override. 0 inherits vector_batch_search_threads.
      --vector-diskann-offline-search-threads=# vector_diskann_offline_search_threads Global Yes Integer 1 0 65535 Searcher thread count used when the DiskANN offline adapter loads a disk index. 0 uses the adapter default.
      --vector-diskann-search-io-limit=# vector_diskann_search_io_limit Global Yes Integer 0 0 4294967295 Search I/O operation limit used when the DiskANN offline adapter loads a disk index. 0 means no MySQL-imposed limit.
      --vector-diskann-cache-nodes=# vector_diskann_cache_nodes Global Yes Integer 0 0 4294967295 BFS static-cache node count used when the DiskANN offline adapter loads a disk index. Nonzero values take precedence and are capped by the index row count; 0 first derives a byte budget from vector_diskann_search_cache_size / vector_diskann_search_cache_ratio, then converts it with the DiskANN cached-node footprint.
      --vector-diskann-search-cache-size=# vector_diskann_search_cache_size Global Yes Integer 0 0 64-bit platforms: 18446744073709551615; 32-bit platforms: 4294967295 bytes Explicit byte budget used to derive the BFS static-cache node count when vector_diskann_cache_nodes=0. 0 derives the byte budget from vector_diskann_search_cache_ratio * raw vector bytes.
      --vector-diskann-search-cache-ratio=# vector_diskann_search_cache_ratio Global Yes Double 0.1 0 1 Raw-vector-byte ratio used to derive the BFS static-cache byte budget when both vector_diskann_cache_nodes=0 and vector_diskann_search_cache_size=0.
      --vector-diskann-search-complexity=# vector_diskann_search_complexity Global Yes Integer 64 1 4294967295 Default query-time search-list size for new DiskANN indexes. The index-level VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_COMPLEXITY() setting takes precedence. This value is not the final SQL topK and is not the exact-rerank returned-candidate count.
      --vector-diskann-search-profile={manual|fast|balanced|high_recall} vector_diskann_search_profile Global Yes Enumeration manual manual, fast, balanced, high_recall DiskANN segmented search profile. manual uses the index-level or global search_complexity, fast favors a lower search list, balanced chooses a middle recall/throughput level by segment fan-out, and high_recall chooses a higher search list by fan-out. This profile only affects DiskANN segmented search. If the user explicitly sets a search complexity higher than the profile-derived value, the explicit value is preserved.
      --vector-diskann-search-beamwidth=# vector_diskann_search_beamwidth Global Yes Integer 16 1 128 DiskANN offline-adapter query beam width. The underlying DiskANN search reads at most 128 sectors per step, so larger values are rejected. Increase search_complexity for recall; do not scale this value with the search list.
      --vector-diskann-exact-rerank-candidates=# vector_diskann_exact_rerank_candidates Global Yes Integer 0 0 1048576 Preferred candidate count per query before DiskANN exact rerank. 0 derives the window from the search list, profile, and topK. Positive values can improve compressed-index recall at higher exact-vector read, CPU, and I/O cost, and the final window is still capped by vector_search_batch_result_count.
      --vector-diskann-pq-code-budget-size=# vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_size Global Yes Integer 0 0 64-bit platforms: 18446744073709551615; 32-bit platforms: 4294967295 bytes Total DiskANN offline-build PQ code budget. 0 derives the budget from vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_ratio.
      --vector-diskann-pq-code-budget-ratio=# vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_ratio Global Yes Double 0.125 0 1 DiskANN PQ-code budget ratio relative to raw vector bytes when vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_size=0.
      --vector-diskann-disk-pq-dims=# vector_diskann_disk_pq_dims Global Yes Integer 0 0 512 Disk PQ dimensions written into the DiskANN offline-build disk layout. 0 means recall-first automatic selection: keep raw vectors when each full-vector node record fits one 4KB DiskANN sector; otherwise choose the largest disk-PQ dimension that fits the sector and the DiskANN C++ loader chunk limit. Nonzero values explicitly set the disk-PQ dimension, with a maximum of 512.
      --vector-diskann-accelerate-build[={OFF|ON}] vector_diskann_accelerate_build Global Yes Boolean OFF OFF, ON Allows DiskANN offline build to use accelerated build paths when the active adapter supports them. Backends without this capability keep the standard build path.
      --vector-diskann-shuffle-build[={OFF|ON}] vector_diskann_shuffle_build Global Yes Boolean OFF OFF, ON Allows DiskANN offline build to shuffle build input batches when the active adapter supports it. The default preserves input order.
      --vector-diskann-use-bfs-cache[={OFF|ON}] vector_diskann_use_bfs_cache Global Yes Boolean OFF OFF, ON Forces DiskANN offline load to use the BFS cache. Even when OFF, BFS cache is selected automatically when search cache nodes are configured.
      --vector-diskann-max-degree=# vector_diskann_max_degree Global Yes Integer 32 1 4294967295 Default graph max degree for new DiskANN indexes. CREATE VECTOR INDEX captures this value before backfill.
      --vector-diskann-build-complexity=# vector_diskann_build_complexity Global Yes Integer 64 1 4294967295 Default build complexity for new DiskANN indexes. CREATE VECTOR INDEX captures this value before backfill.
      --vector-diskann-build-mode={auto|serial|offline} vector_diskann_build_mode Global Yes Enumeration auto auto, serial, offline Default DiskANN build / rebuild path for indexes without an explicit index-level mode. The index-level setting takes precedence.
      --vector-diskann-pq-runtime={official|native_auto|native_strict} vector_diskann_pq_runtime Global Yes Enumeration native_auto official, native_auto, native_strict DiskANN PQ/kmeans runtime selector. native_auto prefers MySQL-Vector native PQ artifacts and may fall back to the official path; native_strict uses the same native path without fallback; official uses only the upstream DiskANN PQ/kmeans path.
      --vector-index-consistency-mode={transactional|standalone} vector_index_consistency_mode Global Yes Enumeration transactional transactional, standalone Default consistency path for named indexes without an explicit consistency setting. The statement-level setting takes precedence. Table-bound mapped indexes always use transactional.
      --vector-entry-cache-size=# vector_entry_cache_size Global Yes Integer 268435456 0 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) bytes SQL-layer committed vector payload cache budget. 0 disables committed payload caching and reads through to the truth-store on demand. Standalone ingest reuses this value as its in-memory flush threshold, where 0 flushes each change to a segment immediately. Standalone non-raw-direct fallback rebuild also checks this value as a materialization budget, but in that path 0 means unlimited materialization. Very small nonzero values can make compact/replay rebuilds fail. Raw-only DiskANN file-native rebuild does not materialize vector payloads.
      --vector-pending-cache-size=# vector_pending_cache_size Global Yes Integer 67108864 0 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) bytes Global in-memory budget for uncommitted vector payloads. 0 means no MySQL-layer pending-cache limit. Payloads beyond a nonzero budget are written to transaction-local spill files. Very small nonzero values greatly reduce cache usefulness; at least 1M is recommended.
      --vector-build-memory-size=# vector_build_memory_size Global Yes Integer 0 0 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) bytes Process-wide root budget shared by all concurrent vector builds in one mysqld. 0 derives it from cgroup v2, cgroup v1, or host resources. An explicit nonzero value is still clipped by the current safe headroom. A nonzero build-memory request requires a complete grant: it fails immediately when it exceeds the current effective root budget and is never allowed to continue with a partial grant. A concurrent build waits only when its request is satisfiable but temporarily held by other leases.
      --vector-build-memory-reserve-size=# vector_build_memory_reserve_size Global Yes Integer 268435456 0 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) bytes Safety memory kept outside the vector-build root budget. Both automatic and explicit root budgets subtract this value. 0 disables the extra reserve.
      --vector-build-resource-wait-timeout=# vector_build_resource_wait_timeout Global Yes Integer 0 0 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) seconds Maximum time to wait for process-wide vector build resources. 0 means an infinite wait. Infinite waits still poll for statement KILL and server shutdown; a nonzero timeout fails the current build when it expires. A request fails immediately instead of waiting when the effective CPU or memory envelope is zero.
      --vector-diskann-build-memory-size=# vector_diskann_build_memory_size Global Yes Integer 1073741824 0 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) bytes Per-build memory-request ceiling for DiskANN offline builds. 0 lets the process-wide resource manager derive the request from the build size and current effective root budget. DiskANN uses only the complete lease actually granted by that manager and does not probe or bypass the process-wide budget again. The native_auto PQ runtime uses the lease to bound training samples, artifacts, worker scratch space, and encode blocks.
      --vector-diskann-raw-segment-size=# vector_diskann_raw_segment_size Global Yes Integer 268435456 1048576 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) bytes DiskANN offline raw segment rollover threshold. A new segment is created after the current segment reaches this size.
      --vector-faiss-train-size=# vector_faiss_train_size Global Yes Integer 0 0 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) bytes FAISS IVF/PQ training sample budget. 0 means the full training set is used.
      --vector-faiss-keep-loaded[={OFF|ON}] vector_faiss_keep_loaded Global Yes Boolean ON OFF, ON Keeps FAISS external serving indexes loaded after snapshot writes. Disable it only for diagnostics that exercise snapshot fallback search.
      --vector-hnsw-index-memory-size=# vector_hnsw_index_memory_size Global Yes Integer 0 0 2^64-1 (64-bit platforms); 2^32-1 (32-bit platforms) bytes hnswlib native index estimated memory limit. 0 disables the pre-build estimate check.
      --vector-lazy-external-runtime[={OFF|ON}] vector_lazy_external_runtime Global Yes Boolean OFF OFF, ON Unloads the external serving runtime after build or recovery and reloads it lazily on the next search. This lowers idle resident memory, while the first query pays the load cost.
      --innodb-vector-upgrade-require-clean-shutdown[={OFF|ON}] innodb_vector_upgrade_require_clean_shutdown Global No Boolean OFF OFF, ON Startup guard that rejects vector DD upgrade when redo recovery is required.

      Actual execution example:

      mysql> SELECT @@global.vector_hnsw_build_threads AS hnsw_threads,
            @@global.vector_faiss_build_threads AS faiss_threads,
            @@global.vector_diskann_build_threads AS diskann_threads,
            @@global.vector_diskann_build_blas_threads AS diskann_blas_threads,
            @@global.vector_diskann_build_mode AS diskann_mode,
            @@global.innodb_vector_upgrade_require_clean_shutdown AS clean_shutdown_required;
+--------------+---------------+-----------------+----------------------+--------------+-------------------------+
| hnsw_threads | faiss_threads | diskann_threads | diskann_blas_threads | diskann_mode | clean_shutdown_required |
+--------------+---------------+-----------------+----------------------+--------------+-------------------------+
|            0 |             0 |               0 |                    1 | auto         |                       0 |
+--------------+---------------+-----------------+----------------------+--------------+-------------------------+

mysql> SET GLOBAL vector_hnsw_build_threads = 4;

mysql> SELECT @@global.vector_hnsw_build_threads AS hnsw_threads;
+--------------+
| hnsw_threads |
+--------------+
|            4 |
+--------------+

mysql> SET GLOBAL vector_hnsw_build_threads = 65536;
ERROR 42000: Variable 'vector_hnsw_build_threads' can't be set to the value of '65536'

mysql> SET GLOBAL innodb_vector_upgrade_require_clean_shutdown = ON;
ERROR HY000: Variable 'innodb_vector_upgrade_require_clean_shutdown' is a read only variable

      vector_faiss_build_threads, vector_diskann_build_threads, and vector_diskann_build_blas_threads have the same visibility, dynamic update semantics, and 0..65535 range validation as vector_hnsw_build_threads. Statement-level build_threads overrides the corresponding global variable when it is greater than 0; statement-level 0 inherits the global variable. vector_diskann_build_blas_threads only controls the inner PQ and numeric-library workers used by DiskANN offline build and is not written to index metadata. The default 1 is the safe value for large offline builds. vector_diskann_build_mode has the same visibility and dynamic update semantics, and accepts auto, serial, and offline. vector_diskann_pq_runtime is also global and dynamic, and accepts official, native_auto, and native_strict. The default native_auto generates MySQL-Vector native PQ artifacts and attempts to consume them through the native PQ graph/cache bridge, while official keeps the upstream DiskANN PQ/kmeans path for debugging and comparison. native_auto can automatically reduce workers or encode block rows within the memory budget and may fall back to the official path; native_strict performs the same budget reductions but fails the build instead of falling back when file layout/checksum, ordinal/doc-id mapping, load, or search-result-shape hard validation fails. A fixed sample missing itself from the approximate-search topK remains diagnostic evidence and does not by itself fail the build. Both native modes generate separate zero-mean Memory-PQ artifacts for graph navigation and uncentered Disk-PQ artifacts with a zero centroid for disk reranking. They reuse compressed codes when both chunk layouts match and train independently when the layouts differ, preventing coordinate-system mismatches on non-zero-mean data. Inspect native_pq_runtime_effective_threads, native_pq_runtime_encode_block_rows, native_pq_runtime_memory_estimate, native_pq_runtime_memory_budget, and native_pq_runtime_memory_adjustment to see the actual selection.

      The logical unit for *_size variables is bytes. Configuration files and startup options support the usual MySQL K / KB / M / MB / G / GB suffixes; runtime SET GLOBAL and SET PERSIST use numeric byte values. Actual execution example:

      mysql> SHOW GLOBAL VARIABLES LIKE 'vector\\_%\\_size';
+------------------------------------------+------------+
| Variable_name                            | Value      |
+------------------------------------------+------------+
| vector_build_memory_reserve_size         | 268435456  |
| vector_build_memory_size                 | 0          |
| vector_build_pipeline_min_size           | 4294967296 |
| vector_build_segment_target_size         | 2147483648 |
| vector_diskann_build_memory_size         | 1073741824 |
| vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_size       | 0          |
| vector_diskann_raw_segment_size          | 268435456  |
| vector_diskann_search_cache_size         | 0          |
| vector_entry_cache_size                  | 268435456  |
| vector_faiss_train_size                  | 0          |
| vector_hnsw_index_memory_size            | 0          |
| vector_pending_cache_size                | 67108864   |
+------------------------------------------+------------+

mysql> SET GLOBAL vector_entry_cache_size = 1048576;

mysql> SET GLOBAL vector_entry_cache_size = -1;
ERROR 42000: Variable 'vector_entry_cache_size' can't be set to the value of '-1'

      0.2 Upgrade guard parameter

      When upgrading from a version without the vector data dictionary, shut down the old version cleanly once before starting the vector version:

      SET GLOBAL innodb_fast_shutdown = 0;

      Then shut down the old version normally and start the vector version.

      The vector version provides this startup option:

      innodb_vector_upgrade_require_clean_shutdown = ON

      The default is OFF, so the original MySQL upgrade behavior is unchanged. If set to ON, the server checks whether this startup performed InnoDB redo recovery before upgrading to the vector data dictionary version. If the old data directory requires redo recovery, the upgrade is rejected and the user must return to the old version, shut it down cleanly with innodb_fast_shutdown=0, and then upgrade again.

      Usage recommendations:

      • During production upgrade windows, explicitly set --innodb-vector-upgrade-require-clean-shutdown=ON to block direct upgrade to the vector version after an abnormal old-version shutdown.
      • If the existing upgrade process already guarantees a clean old-version shutdown, this option can stay at the default OFF.
      • This option is a startup-time read-only parameter. It cannot be changed at runtime and is not persisted by SET PERSIST.

      1. Vector columns

      1.1 Syntax

      VECTOR(dim)
      • dim must be a positive integer in the range 1..1073741823.
      • SHOW CREATE TABLE, SHOW COLUMNS, DESCRIBE, and INFORMATION_SCHEMA all display the type as VECTOR(dim).

      Example:

      CREATE TABLE t_vec (
id BIGINT PRIMARY KEY,
emb VECTOR(4)
) ENGINE=InnoDB;

      1.2 Write recommendations

      • For text literals, consistently convert through VEC_FROMTEXT('[1,2,3]') first.
      • The written value dimension must match the column definition, otherwise an error is returned.
      • Every vector element must be a finite float32 value. Column writes, scalar functions, index writes, and index searches reject NaN, positive infinity, and negative infinity.

      2. Basic vector functions and enum parameters

      The examples in this section come from mysql --table --raw runs against an isolated local instance built from the current branch's 8.0.46-debug binary. The client returned the floating-point values exactly as shown.

      2.1 Enum parameters

      metric, mode, and provider parsing is case-insensitive, and leading or trailing whitespace is ignored.

      Enum Values Meaning
      metric EUCLIDEAN, L2 Euclidean distance. Lower is more similar.
      metric COSINE 1 - cosine_similarity. Lower is more similar. Zero vectors are invalid.
      metric IP, INNER_PRODUCT Negative inner product in VEC_DISTANCE() so ascending distance order still means more similar first.
      mode MEMORY In-memory serving index.
      mode EXTERNAL External/native persistent backend mode.
      provider NATIVE Debug-only exact backend for tests and debugging when at least one of FAISS, DiskANN, or HNSW is compiled; unavailable in Release builds.
      provider HNSWLIB, HNSW hnswlib backend. Supports MEMORY.
      provider FAISS Faiss backend.
      provider DISKANN DiskANN backend. Supports EXTERNAL.

      Unsupported mode/provider combinations fail at index creation time, for example DISKANN/MEMORY and HNSWLIB/EXTERNAL.

      When provider is omitted, MySQL-Vector uses the global vector_default_library. Its compile-time default chooses the first compiled library in diskann, hnsw, faiss order. If the variable is empty, provider-omitted index creation fails. When mode is also omitted, MySQL-Vector first resolves the provider and then chooses a compatible default mode: DISKANN and FAISS use EXTERNAL, while HNSWLIB uses MEMORY. Explicit mode/provider combinations are still validated strictly. NATIVE is available only in Debug builds with at least one real vector library compiled and never participates in the global default.

      2.2 Basic function summary

      Function Behavior Input parameters Return value
      VEC_FROMTEXT(text) Encodes a text vector literal into MySQL internal vector bytes. text: a vector literal such as '[1,2,3]'. Binary float32 vector bytes.
      VEC_TOTEXT(vector_expr) Decodes internal vector bytes into text. vector_expr: a binary vector value. Text vector literal.
      VECTOR_DIM(vector_expr) Returns vector dimension. vector_expr: a binary vector value. Number of vector elements.
      VEC_NORMALIZE(vector_expr) Returns an L2-normalized vector. vector_expr: a non-zero binary vector value. Normalized binary vector bytes.
      VEC_DOT_PRODUCT(lhs, rhs) Computes dot product. lhs and rhs: same-dimension binary vector values. sum(lhs[i] * rhs[i]).
      VEC_INNER_PRODUCT(lhs, rhs) Computes inner product. lhs and rhs: same-dimension binary vector values. Raw inner product; currently equivalent to VEC_DOT_PRODUCT().
      VEC_DISTANCE_EUCLIDEAN(lhs, rhs) Computes Euclidean distance. lhs and rhs: same-dimension binary vector values. sqrt(sum((lhs-rhs)^2)).
      VEC_DISTANCE_COSINE(lhs, rhs) Computes cosine distance. lhs and rhs: same-dimension non-zero binary vector values. 1 - cosine_similarity.
      VEC_DISTANCE(lhs, rhs[, metric]) Computes distance with the selected metric. lhs and rhs: same-dimension binary vector values; optional metric uses the enum values in section 2.1. Distance value; IP / INNER_PRODUCT returns negative inner product to preserve ascending distance order.

      Notes:

      • The direct return value of VEC_FROMTEXT() is the internal binary vector. Wrap it in HEX(...) or VEC_TOTEXT(...) for manual inspection.
      • VEC_NORMALIZE() rejects a zero vector.
      • For text literals, convert explicitly with VEC_FROMTEXT(...). Index search functions also accept text literals, but explicit conversion makes client encoding issues easier to diagnose.

      Debug builds also register VEC_DEBUG_TRUTH_STORE_GET_HEX() and VEC_DEBUG_TRUTH_STORE_SET_HEX() for truth-store verification. Registration is controlled only by NDEBUG, not by whether MTR is enabled. They are diagnostic helpers, not normal application APIs.

      2.3 Basic function details and examples

      VEC_FROMTEXT(text)

      Parses a text vector literal such as '[1,2,3]' into the internal VECTOR binary representation. Elements are stored as float32; use HEX() or VEC_TOTEXT() when inspecting the value manually.

      mysql> SELECT HEX(VEC_FROMTEXT('[1,2,3]')) AS vec_fromtext_hex;
    -> 0000803F0000004000004040

      VEC_TOTEXT(vector_expr)

      Formats internal vector bytes back to a text vector literal for display, manual verification, or diagnostics.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_TOTEXT(VEC_FROMTEXT('[1,2,3]')) AS vec_totext;
    -> [1,2,3]

      VECTOR_DIM(vector_expr)

      Returns the number of elements in the vector. The value should match the VECTOR(dim) column definition or the index creation dimension.

      mysql> SELECT VECTOR_DIM(VEC_FROMTEXT('[1,2,3]')) AS vector_dim;
    -> 3

      VEC_NORMALIZE(vector_expr)

      Returns the L2-normalized vector. The input must be non-zero because a zero vector has no defined direction.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_TOTEXT(VEC_NORMALIZE(VEC_FROMTEXT('[3,4]'))) AS normalized;
    -> [0.6000000238418579,0.800000011920929]

      VEC_DOT_PRODUCT(lhs, rhs)

      Computes sum(lhs[i] * rhs[i]) for two same-dimension vectors. Different dimensions return an error.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_DOT_PRODUCT(VEC_FROMTEXT('[1,2,3]'), VEC_FROMTEXT('[4,5,6]')) AS dot_product;
    -> 32

      VEC_INNER_PRODUCT(lhs, rhs)

      Computes raw inner product for two same-dimension vectors. It is currently equivalent to VEC_DOT_PRODUCT(), but differs from the negative inner-product distance returned by VEC_DISTANCE(..., 'IP').

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INNER_PRODUCT(VEC_FROMTEXT('[1,2,3]'), VEC_FROMTEXT('[4,5,6]')) AS inner_product;
    -> 32

      VEC_DISTANCE_EUCLIDEAN(lhs, rhs)

      Computes Euclidean, or L2, distance for same-dimension vectors. Lower values are more similar.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_DISTANCE_EUCLIDEAN(VEC_FROMTEXT('[0,0]'), VEC_FROMTEXT('[3,4]')) AS l2_distance;
    -> 5

      VEC_DISTANCE_COSINE(lhs, rhs)

      Computes 1 - cosine_similarity. Inputs must be same-dimension non-zero vectors.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_DISTANCE_COSINE(VEC_FROMTEXT('[1,0]'), VEC_FROMTEXT('[0,1]')) AS cosine_distance;
    -> 1

      VEC_DISTANCE(lhs, rhs[, metric])

      Computes distance using the selected metric. L2/EUCLIDEAN returns Euclidean distance, COSINE returns cosine distance, and IP/INNER_PRODUCT returns negative inner product.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_DISTANCE(VEC_FROMTEXT('[0,0]'), VEC_FROMTEXT('[3,4]'), 'L2') AS distance_l2;
    -> 5
mysql> SELECT VEC_DISTANCE(VEC_FROMTEXT('[1,2]'), VEC_FROMTEXT('[3,4]'), 'IP') AS distance_ip;
    -> -11

      Full actual execution examples for basic functions:

      mysql> SELECT HEX(VEC_FROMTEXT('[1,2,3]')) AS vec_fromtext_hex;
+--------------------------+
| vec_fromtext_hex         |
+--------------------------+
| 0000803F0000004000004040 |
+--------------------------+

+------------+

+------------+

      3. Optional vector index modes

      MySQL Vector index behavior is determined by run mode, import method, build mode, and backend library. Run mode defines the consistency boundary, import method defines how data enters the index, build mode defines how DiskANN constructs the index graph structure, and backend library selects the ANN implementation.

      3.1 Run modes

      Option Meaning Recommended use
      transactional Writes truth-store rows and uses InnoDB-persisted truth as the source of data, participating in commit, rollback, recovery, and table-level DDL lifecycle. Production table indexes, transaction consistency, replication, crash recovery, or MVCC visibility.
      standalone Independent named index. It does not write truth-store rows and does not provide rollback or MVCC. Writes enter the ingest store and become searchable after explicit rebuild. Benchmarks, offline builds, or independent ANN indexes that can tolerate loss of unpublished data.

      3.2 Import methods

      Option Meaning Recommended use
      Single-row SQL Writes one row at a time through normal table DML, VEC_INDEX_UPSERT(), or VEC_INDEX_ERASE(). Online small-batch writes, functional validation, and application paths interleaved with normal transactions.
      LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT CSV Bulk-loads doc_id and text vectors from a CSV file using MySQL file privilege and format checks. Readable data files, offline imports, and tests where inspecting input content matters.
      LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT FBIN Bulk-loads binary vector and docid files, avoiding text parsing overhead. Large benchmarks, pre-generated client data, and offline imports that need higher ingest throughput.
      VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH() Passes contiguous binary vectors and doc_id arrays through one SQL call. Small to medium client batches where reducing per-row SQL round trips matters.

      3.3 Build modes

      Option Meaning Recommended use
      auto DiskANN default: prefer the offline adapter. It may fall back to serial when offline support is unavailable or the input is too small for the minimum offline graph; other offline build failures do not automatically downgrade. For non-DiskANN backends this is only a default placeholder. Default recommendation when the environment should choose the best available DiskANN path automatically.
      serial Forces DiskANN to use the official Garnet serial insert ABI. Compatibility checks, small data sets, or isolating offline-adapter issues.
      offline Writes raw DiskANN input first and then calls the offline adapter. Missing adapter fails explicitly. Large DiskANN imports or rebuilds where a build failure must not disturb the old serving index.

      3.4 Native runtime scheduling and adaptive build pipeline

      The MySQL-Vector Native Runtime coordinates standalone build/search paths for HNSWLIB, Faiss, and DiskANN. It owns input segmentation, thread budgeting, segment task scheduling, observability, and required multi-segment result merging; the actual ANN graph build and search still run inside the selected third-party backend.

      MySQL-Vector has two build pipeline paths: direct and segmented. Small inputs use the direct path by default and pass the current input to one backend build. Large inputs or multiple raw segments use the segmented path by default. HNSW/Faiss can split input into multiple segment serving handles and merge fan-out search results. DiskANN standalone raw segments also keep one backend per raw segment by default; search fans out to all segments and then globally merges the final topK. Set vector_diskann_segmented_serving=OFF only to merge raw segments into one logical manifest and one offline serving handle for diagnostics.

      LOAD VECTOR / BULK / transactional rebuild
  -> input stats(row_count, payload_size, raw_segment_count)
  -> vector_build_pipeline_mode
     -> direct: one backend build
     -> segmented:
         -> DiskANN raw-only default:
            raw segments -> one backend per segment -> fan-out search / merge
         -> DiskANN raw-only with vector_diskann_segmented_serving=OFF:
            raw segments -> single logical manifest -> one offline serving handle
         -> HNSW/Faiss: raw segments -> parallel build task(s) -> segmented serving handles
         -> multi-handle search: segment fan-out -> expanded candidates -> global topK merge

      Selection rules:

      • vector_build_pipeline_mode=direct: force the direct path.
      • vector_build_pipeline_mode=segmented: force the segmented pipeline.
      • vector_build_pipeline_mode=auto: choose segmented when the raw segment count is greater than 1, the row count reaches vector_build_pipeline_min_rows, or payload bytes reach vector_build_pipeline_min_size; otherwise choose direct.
      • vector_build_pipeline_min_rows=0 or vector_build_pipeline_min_size=0 disables the corresponding trigger.
      • LOAD VECTOR DATA derives the effective row limit from vector_build_segment_target_size, applies the vector_build_segment_max_rows safety cap, and then rolls raw segments.
      • vector_diskann_segment_profile only affects the DiskANN standalone offline raw-only path. manual uses the explicit segment variables, throughput favors larger segments, balanced uses about a 512MiB target, and high_recall uses about a 256MiB target for high-dimensional data. Non-DiskANN paths, transactional paths, non-offline builds, and vector_build_pipeline_mode=direct do not apply this profile.
      • vector_diskann_search_profile only affects DiskANN segmented search. manual uses the index-level or global search_complexity, fast favors throughput, balanced raises the search list to a middle level for lower segment fan-out, and high_recall raises it to a higher level for lower segment fan-out. If an explicit search_complexity is higher than the profile-derived value, the explicit value is preserved.

      Segment sizing is a tradeoff among build cost, recovery granularity, and recall. HNSW/Faiss/DiskANN multi-segment fan-out may improve recall, but it increases build, search fan-out, and merge cost. DiskANN raw-only standalone/offline fans out by default and falls back to single-index build only when vector_diskann_segmented_serving=OFF is set explicitly. Once the segmented path is selected, vector_build_pipeline_max_tasks is the upper bound for outer segment-build concurrency; vector_*_build_threads and statement-level build_threads represent the total thread budget for this index build. The scheduler divides that budget across active segment tasks and reports the per-segment value as scheduler_effective_build_threads, avoiding CPU scheduler storms when multiple high-thread builds run together. For DiskANN, a nonzero vector_diskann_build_memory_size also bounds outer segment task concurrency: the scheduler estimates memory from the segment payload and reduces active tasks when the budget is too small. Use scheduler_segment_memory_budget, scheduler_segment_memory_estimate, and scheduler_segment_parallel_reason to check whether memory pruning was applied. For large benchmarks, start with vector_diskann_segment_profile=balanced or the default manual profile plus a 2GiB target size to establish throughput. If exact recall is below target, switch to vector_diskann_search_profile=high_recall or reduce the segment target. Adjust segment size or disable vector_diskann_segmented_serving only for recovery-granularity validation, matching a specific external segment layout, or recall diagnostics.

      Fallback flow is mode-specific:

      Standalone:
  raw-only LOAD VECTOR DATA
    -> provider raw-direct backend
    -> DiskANN: file-native offline build when available
    -> HNSW/Faiss: compact reader input and bulk rebuild
    -> no matching provider/runtime: fail; no transactional fallback

Transactional:
  truth-store committed snapshot
    -> raw/doc-id export for offline-capable DiskANN
    -> provider direct backend build
    -> explicit offline failure keeps the old serving backend
    -> auto may fall back to serial when offline is unavailable or input is too small
    -> other offline build failures do not automatically fall back

      Use VEC_INDEX_INFO() to check the actual path. Important fields include build_pipeline_decision, build_pipeline_raw_segments, backend_variant, scheduler_path, scheduler_segment_count, scheduler_task_count, scheduler_cpu_budget, scheduler_effective_build_threads, scheduler_effective_blas_threads, scheduler_effective_segment_tasks, scheduler_segment_memory_estimate, scheduler_segment_memory_budget, scheduler_segment_parallel_reason, build_segment_profile, build_segment_profile_reason, build_segment_effective_row_limit, build_segment_effective_target_size, build_segment_policy, diskann_segment_pq_chunks, diskann_segment_cache_nodes, diskann_segment_build_wall_ms, diskann_segment_fallback_reason, diskann_pq_runtime, native_pq_runtime_selected_path, native_pq_runtime_raw_reader_ms, native_pq_runtime_train_ms, native_pq_runtime_encode_ms, native_pq_runtime_train_rows, native_pq_runtime_compressed_rows, native_pq_runtime_artifact_validation_ms, native_pq_runtime_centroid_scan_kernel, native_pq_runtime_artifacts_written, native_pq_runtime_artifacts_consumed, native_pq_runtime_official_pq_used, and native_pq_runtime_bridge, native_pq_runtime_bridge_ms, native_pq_runtime_graph_ms, native_pq_runtime_cache_ms, and native_pq_runtime_artifact_validation, plus the self-hit quality diagnostics native_pq_runtime_validation_failed, native_pq_runtime_validation_failed_doc_id, native_pq_runtime_validation_best_doc_id, native_pq_runtime_validation_result_count, distance fields, and artifact checksum fields.

      SELECT JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.scheduler_path')) AS scheduler_path,
       JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.scheduler_segment_count') AS segments,
       JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.scheduler_effective_build_threads') AS build_threads,
       JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.diskann_pq_runtime')) AS diskann_pq_runtime,
       JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.native_pq_runtime_train_ms') AS train_ms,
       JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.native_pq_runtime_encode_ms') AS encode_ms,
       JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.native_pq_runtime_centroid_scan_kernel')) AS scan_kernel,
       JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.native_pq_runtime_artifacts_written') AS native_artifacts,
       JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.native_pq_runtime_artifacts_consumed') AS native_consumed,
       JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.native_pq_runtime_official_pq_used') AS official_pq_used,
       JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.native_pq_runtime_bridge')) AS native_bridge,
       JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx'), '$.native_pq_runtime_artifact_validation')) AS artifact_validation;
scheduler_path	segments	build_threads	diskann_pq_runtime	train_ms	encode_ms	scan_kernel	native_artifacts	native_consumed	official_pq_used	native_bridge	artifact_validation
segmented	4	4	native_strict	325843	8244	avx512	1	1	0	native_pq_graph_cache	ok

      Example output for related build-pipeline variables from an executed test path:

      mysql> SELECT VARIABLE_NAME, VARIABLE_VALUE
    -> FROM performance_schema.global_variables
    -> WHERE VARIABLE_NAME IN ('vector_build_pipeline_mode',
    ->                         'vector_diskann_segment_profile',
    ->                         'vector_build_pipeline_min_rows',
    ->                         'vector_build_pipeline_min_size',
    ->                         'vector_build_segment_max_rows',

    ->                         'vector_build_pipeline_progress_interval')
    -> ORDER BY VARIABLE_NAME;
VARIABLE_NAME	VARIABLE_VALUE
vector_build_pipeline_max_tasks	1
vector_build_pipeline_min_rows	1048576
vector_build_pipeline_min_size	4294967296
vector_build_pipeline_mode	auto
vector_build_pipeline_progress_interval	10
vector_build_segment_max_rows	4294967295
vector_build_segment_target_size	2147483648
vector_diskann_segment_profile	manual

mysql> SET GLOBAL vector_build_segment_max_rows = 1;
mysql> SET GLOBAL vector_build_segment_target_size = 1048576;
mysql> SELECT JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx_load_segmented'),
    ->                    '$.standalone_raw_segment_count') AS raw_segment_count;
raw_segment_count
3

mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_BULK_BUILD('idx_load_segmented');
VEC_INDEX_BULK_BUILD('idx_load_segmented')
1

mysql> SELECT JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx_load_segmented'),
    ->                                  '$.build_pipeline_decision')) AS decision,
    ->        JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx_load_segmented'),
    ->                     '$.build_pipeline_raw_segments') AS raw_segments,
    ->        JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx_load_segmented'),
    ->                     '$.build_segment_policy')) AS segment_policy;

segmented	3	row_limited

      3.5 Backend libraries

      Option Meaning Recommended use
      NATIVE Debug-only exact backend with linear scan. Only for smoke tests and correctness baselines when a Debug build includes at least one real vector library; not for production indexes.
      HNSWLIB In-memory HNSW ANN. Standalone uses the MySQL-Vector native runtime with the direct hnswlib backend. In-memory indexes, low-latency approximate search, and small to medium data sets.
      FAISS Faiss HNSW/IVF/IVFPQ with build/search thread controls. IVF/PQ variants, Faiss ecosystem compatibility, or CPU SIMD acceleration.
      DiskANN SSD/external ANN with serial and offline paths. Standalone uses the MySQL-Vector native runtime with the DiskANN adapter. Large data sets where disk-backed indexes help control memory use.

      3.6 Mode / import / build support matrix

      The table below reflects the current executable paths. The auto, serial, and offline build modes have operational differences only for DiskANN. HNSWLIB, FAISS, and NATIVE show auto only as the unified field's default value.

      Run mode Import method Build mode Backend / library Supported Default path Fallback path Notes
      standalone Single-row SQL, LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT CSV, LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT FBIN, VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH() auto NATIVE Debug with at least one real vector library In-memory or external exact backend for tests and debugging only None Unavailable in Release builds or Debug builds without a real vector library; production deployments should use HNSWLIB, FAISS, or DISKANN.
      standalone Single-row SQL, LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT CSV, LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT FBIN, VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH() auto HNSWLIB Yes MySQL-Vector native runtime with the direct hnswlib backend Create/rebuild fails when the direct backend is not compiled; no transactional fallback Standalone writes use deferred-build semantics and are not searchable until explicit rebuild succeeds.
      standalone Single-row SQL, LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT CSV, LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT FBIN, VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH() auto FAISS Yes MySQL-Vector native runtime with the direct Faiss backend Create/rebuild fails when the direct backend is not compiled; no transactional fallback FAISS build/search threads are controlled by vector_faiss_build_threads and vector_faiss_search_threads.
      standalone Single-row SQL, LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT CSV, LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT FBIN, VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH() auto DiskANN Yes MySQL-Vector native runtime preferring the DiskANN offline adapter auto can fall back to the official Garnet serial ABI when offline support is unavailable or the input is too small; other offline failures and explicit offline do not fall back to serial For large standalone DiskANN imports, prefer Linux + offline mode. Build the offline adapter from MYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_ROOT.
      standalone Any standalone import method serial DiskANN Yes Official Garnet serial insert ABI Fails when no Garnet runtime is available Compatibility and diagnostic path; slower than offline builds.
      standalone Any standalone import method offline DiskANN Yes MySQL DiskANN offline adapter Fails explicitly when the adapter is missing Search is unavailable until import and rebuild complete successfully.
      transactional Normal table DML, VEC_INDEX_UPSERT(), VEC_INDEX_ERASE() auto NATIVE / HNSWLIB / FAISS Yes Write truth-store rows, then rebuild or apply the serving backend for the provider Creation or recovery fails when the provider is not compiled. A compiled HNSWLIB provider may use its internal exact fallback when native state is unavailable; a compiled FAISS external path uses its persisted snapshot fallback only for supported recovery/search failures. The truth-store is the source of truth for commit, recovery, and MVCC visibility.
      transactional LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT CSV, LOAD VECTOR DATA FORMAT FBIN auto NATIVE / HNSWLIB / FAISS Yes Bulk reader writes truth-store rows and rebuilds the serving backend Provider-specific direct fallback rules apply CSV uses text vector parsing. FBIN is the high-throughput binary import path.
      transactional VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH() auto NATIVE / HNSWLIB / FAISS Yes SQL bulk reader writes truth-store rows and rebuilds or marks the serving backend Provider-specific direct fallback rules apply Duplicate doc ids use replace semantics.
      transactional Normal table DML, LOAD VECTOR DATA, VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH() auto DiskANN Yes Truth-store streaming reader writes a raw manifest and then attempts the offline adapter auto can fall back to the official Garnet serial ABI when offline is unavailable or the input is too small; other offline failures do not downgrade and keep the truth-store and old serving index Use for DiskANN bulk imports that need transactional consistency.
      transactional Normal table DML, LOAD VECTOR DATA, VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH() serial DiskANN Yes Committed truth-store rows are inserted one by one into the official Garnet serial ABI Fails when the Garnet runtime is unavailable; truth-store data is preserved Use for small data sets or compatibility diagnostics.
      transactional Normal table DML, LOAD VECTOR DATA, VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH() offline DiskANN Yes truth-store streaming reader -> raw manifest -> DiskANN offline adapter Fails explicitly when the adapter is missing; truth-store data and the old serving index are preserved Explicit offline does not automatically downgrade to serial.

      4. Independent named index functions

      Independent named indexes are created by VEC_INDEX_CREATE(). They are not bound to table columns and do not appear in SHOW CREATE TABLE. Their consistency mode can be transactional or standalone. Callers explicitly perform UPSERT, ERASE, transaction commit, rebuild, and recovery.

      The current default schema at creation time is persisted as the independent index owner schema. Creating the index requires CREATE on that schema; writing, bulk importing, tuning, rebuilding, or recovering requires ALTER; dropping requires DROP; and information, single-search, and batch-search operations require SELECT. Changing the current default schema later neither changes ownership nor bypasses authorization. Table-bound index operations continue to check privileges on the bound table.

      Defaults:

      • metric='euclidean'
      • Omitted provider uses global vector_default_library. The compile-time default chooses the first compiled library in diskann, hnsw, faiss order; if none is compiled, it is empty and provider-omitted index creation fails. Startup options or SET GLOBAL attempts that select an uncompiled library raise an error.
      • Omitted mode is chosen after provider resolution: DISKANN and FAISS use EXTERNAL, and HNSWLIB uses MEMORY. Explicit modes must remain compatible with the selected provider.
      • NATIVE can only be selected explicitly in Debug builds with at least one real vector library for tests and debugging; it is not used by vector_default_library.

      4.1 Lifecycle, write, and query function summary

      Function Behavior Input parameters Return value
      VEC_INDEX_CREATE(index_name, dim[, metric[, mode[, provider[, build_threads[, consistency_mode]]]]]) Creates an independent named vector index with transactional or standalone consistency. index_name: index name; dim: dimension in 1..1073741823; optional metric, mode, and provider use the enum values in section 2.1; build_threads: HNSWLIB, FAISS, or DiskANN build / rebuild worker count in 0..65535, where 0 inherits the corresponding provider global variable; consistency_mode: optional transactional or standalone, inherited from global vector_index_consistency_mode when omitted. 1 on success; errors on failure.
      VEC_INDEX_DROP(index_name) Drops an independent named vector index. index_name: index name. 1 when dropped; 0 when the index does not exist. Other drop failures raise an error.
      VEC_INDEX_UPSERT(index_name, doc_id, vector_expr) Inserts or replaces one vector in an index. In standalone mode, this writes only the ingest store and does not update the serving backend before an explicit rebuild. index_name: index name; doc_id: unsigned 64-bit document id; vector_expr: vector value. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH(index_name, doc_id_blob, vector_blob, row_count) Batch-inserts or replaces multiple document vectors. Transactional indexes write the entire statement batch to the truth-store; standalone indexes write the ingest store and do not update the serving backend before an explicit rebuild. doc_id_blob: contiguous little-endian uint64 document ids; vector_blob: contiguous little-endian float32 vector values; row_count: number of rows in the batch. Number of inserted rows. row_count must be greater than 0; invalid lengths and non-finite floating-point values raise an error.
      VEC_INDEX_ERASE(index_name, doc_id) Deletes one vector id from an index. In standalone mode, this writes only the ingest store and does not update the serving backend before an explicit rebuild. index_name: index name; doc_id: unsigned 64-bit document id. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_SEARCH(index_name, query_vector_expr, top_k) Nearest-neighbor search that returns document ids. index_name: index name; query: query vector; top_k: 0..10000, where 0 returns an empty result. JSON text array, such as [1,2].
      VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_BATCH(index_name, query_batch_expr, query_count, top_k) Batch nearest-neighbor search that returns document ids. It may use backend batch parallel search when there are no pending transactions or mapped MVCC table filters. query_batch_expr: contiguous binary concatenation of query_count same-dimension vectors; query_count: 1..vector_search_batch_count, with default limit 1024 and variable maximum 65535; top_k: 0..10000; query_count * top_k must not exceed vector_search_batch_result_count, whose default is 65536 and variable maximum is 1048576. JSON text two-dimensional array, such as [[1,2],[2,1]].
      VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_WITH_DISTANCE(index_name, query_vector_expr, top_k) Returns JSON search results with distances. Same parameters as VEC_INDEX_SEARCH(). JSON text array; each element contains doc_id and distance.
      VEC_INDEX_INFO(index_name) Returns JSON metadata and lifecycle state. index_name: index name. JSON text object.
      VEC_INDEX_LIST() Lists registered vector indexes visible to the current account; unauthorized objects are silently hidden. None. JSON text array containing index names for which the current account has SELECT access.
      VEC_INDEX_BULK_LOAD_BEGIN(index_name) Enters bulk-load mode. Later transactional writes update only committed truth rows, while standalone writes update only the ingest/source store; neither updates the serving backend yet. index_name: index name; the index must not have uncommitted staged changes. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_BULK_BUILD(index_name) Builds a shadow backend from committed truth rows or the standalone ingest/segment source, depending on consistency mode, and atomically installs it after validation. hnswlib uses the configured hnsw_build_threads; Faiss applies faiss_build_threads, and external mode persists a recoverable Faiss snapshot and manifest.
