DiskANN follows diskann_build_mode: auto prefers the vendored runtime/offline adapter and falls back to official serial insert when offline is unavailable or the input is too small; other offline build failures do not automatically downgrade. serial forces official serial insertion, while offline uses only the offline path. DiskANN publishes its persistent artifacts only after a successful build. index_name: index name. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_REBUILD(index_name) Rebuilds one serving index from committed entries or the standalone ingest/segment source, depending on consistency mode. The implementation captures a plan under a short lock, builds the shadow backend outside the lock, then validates and swaps the live backend under a short lock. hnswlib and Faiss can use their configured build workers, and DiskANN follows diskann_build_mode to choose auto/offline/serial build paths. index_name: index name. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_RECOVER(index_name) Normally recovers a transactional index from a recoverable backend artifact or committed entries, and a standalone index from its persisted ingest/segment source. When corrupt committed/changelog state places the registry in recovery_required, it rebuilds the projection from the bound InnoDB table only when exactly one index is affected. It must run as a standalone autocommit statement, not inside an explicit transaction, with autocommit=0, or under LOCK TABLES. Use VEC_INDEX_RECOVER_ALL() when multiple indexes require recovery. index_name: index name. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_REBUILD_ALL() Rebuilds all registered indexes by constructing shadow backends outside the lock and installing them after validation. None. Number of successfully rebuilt indexes.
      VEC_INDEX_RECOVER_ALL() Recovers all registered indexes. In normal state it constructs recovered shadow backends outside the lock and installs them after validation. For corrupt committed/changelog state it write-blocks every bound table before scanning and atomically publishes the complete projection. It must run as a standalone autocommit statement. This local derived-state repair is not binlogged; each replica recovers from its own base tables and artifact health. None. Number of successfully recovered indexes.

      VEC_INDEX_LIST(), VEC_INDEX_SEARCH(), VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_BATCH(), VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_WITH_DISTANCE(), and VEC_INDEX_INFO() return JSON text, not native JSON column values.

      One SQL statement may hold at most one statement-publication operation. Functions that require a publication intent, including create, drop, configuration, rebuild, recovery, and standalone writes, cannot be invoked repeatedly in the same statement or mixed with transactional attached DML. Multiple VEC_INDEX_UPSERT() / VEC_INDEX_ERASE() calls for transactional indexes may appear in one statement and commit atomically as one transaction batch; a later expression error rolls back vector changes already staged by that statement. VEC_INDEX_REBUILD_ALL() and VEC_INDEX_RECOVER_ALL() are themselves single-function entry points.

      Every SQL function that mutates vector state uses MySQL side-effecting Item semantics. The optimizer does not fold a literal-only invocation into one execution or cache its result; the invocation executes once per input row. These functions are prohibited in generated columns, functional indexes, expression defaults, and CHECK constraints.

      4.2 Lifecycle, write, and query function details and examples

      Each per-function example comes from a separate mysql --table --raw run. The examples need not be run in sequence.

      VEC_INDEX_CREATE(index_name, dim[, metric[, mode[, provider[, build_threads[, consistency_mode]]]]])

      Creates an independent named index whose lifecycle is explicitly controlled by the caller. build_threads applies only to HNSWLIB, Faiss, and DiskANN build / rebuild paths; statement-level 0 inherits the global setting. consistency_mode supports transactional and standalone; standalone skips the truth-store and does not provide rollback or MVCC guarantees. Standalone writes first enter the ingest cache and are flushed to a standalone segment manifest when the cache reaches vector_entry_cache_size or when VEC_INDEX_REBUILD() is run explicitly. Flushed segments can be used as rebuild input; unflushed cache contents are not recovered after restart. Raw-only DiskANN can use file-native rebuild. Mixed raw/delta or delta-only fallback paths are constrained by vector_entry_cache_size when they must materialize rebuild input. HNSW, FAISS, and DiskANN standalone indexes all use deferred-build semantics: VEC_INDEX_UPSERT() / VEC_INDEX_ERASE() update only the ingest store and put the index in bulk_loading; search is unavailable until an explicit VEC_INDEX_REBUILD() succeeds. HNSW, FAISS, and DiskANN standalone indexes build and search through the MySQL-Vector native runtime; if the matching direct backend was not compiled or its runtime library is unavailable, the operation returns an error instead of falling back to transactional backends.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_CREATE('idx_fn', 2, 'EUCLIDEAN', 'MEMORY', 'HNSWLIB') AS create_idx_fn;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_DROP(index_name)

      Drops independent named-index metadata and serving state. A successful drop returns 1.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_DROP('idx_fn') AS drop_idx_fn;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_UPSERT(index_name, doc_id, vector_expr)

      Inserts or replaces a document vector. doc_id is an unsigned 64-bit id, and writing the same id again replaces the previous vector.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_UPSERT('idx_fn', 1, VEC_FROMTEXT('[0,0]')) AS upsert_1;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH(index_name, doc_id_blob, vector_blob, row_count)

      Batch-inserts binary vectors into a transactional or standalone independent named index. Transactional indexes write the entire statement batch to the truth-store; standalone indexes write the ingest store and keep the serving backend unchanged until an explicit rebuild. doc_id_blob contains row_count little-endian uint64 values, and vector_blob contains row_count * dimension little-endian float32 values. Use this function for small client-side binary batches; prefer LOAD VECTOR DATA for large file-based imports.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_CREATE('idx_upsert_batch', 2, 'EUCLIDEAN', 'MEMORY',
    ->                         'HNSWLIB', 0, 'standalone');
    -> 1

mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH(
    ->   'idx_upsert_batch',
    ->   X'01000000000000000200000000000000',
    ->   X'0000803f00000000000000000000803f',
    ->   2) AS inserted_rows;
    -> 2

mysql> SELECT JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx_upsert_batch'),
    ->                                  '$.lifecycle_state')) AS lifecycle_state;
    -> bulk_loading

mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_BULK_BUILD('idx_upsert_batch');
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SEARCH(index_name, query_vector_expr, top_k)

      Runs nearest-neighbor search and returns a JSON text array of document ids. top_k=0 returns an empty array.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SEARCH('idx_fn', VEC_FROMTEXT('[0,0]'), 2) AS search_ids;
    -> [1,2]

      VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_BATCH(index_name, query_batch_expr, query_count, top_k)

      Submits multiple same-dimension query vectors in one call and returns a two-dimensional JSON text array. query_batch_expr is the contiguous concatenation of internal VECTOR binary values. The default query_count limit is 1024, and the default total query_count * top_k result-slot limit is 65536; adjust them with vector_search_batch_count and vector_search_batch_result_count.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_BATCH('idx_fn',
    ->        CONCAT(VEC_FROMTEXT('[0,0]'), VEC_FROMTEXT('[3,4]')), 2, 2) AS search_batch_ids;
    -> [[1,2],[2,1]]

      VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_WITH_DISTANCE(index_name, query_vector_expr, top_k)

      Runs nearest-neighbor search and returns document ids together with distances for display or additional filtering.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_WITH_DISTANCE('idx_fn', VEC_FROMTEXT('[0,0]'), 2) AS search_with_distance;
    -> [{"doc_id":1,"distance":0},{"doc_id":2,"distance":5}]

      VEC_INDEX_INFO(index_name)

      Returns JSON text containing index metadata, lifecycle state, backend parameters, and counters. Use JSON_EXTRACT() for automated checks.

      mysql> SELECT JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx_fn'), '$.entry_count') AS info_entry_count;
    -> 2

      VEC_INDEX_LIST()

      Lists standalone and table-bound vector index names visible to the current account as a JSON text array. Table-bound indexes are filtered by SELECT access on the bound table, while independent named indexes are filtered by SELECT access on the persisted owner schema. Unauthorized objects are silently hidden instead of causing the entire listing operation to fail.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_LIST() AS index_list_after_create;
    -> ["idx_fn"]

      VEC_INDEX_ERASE(index_name, doc_id)

      Deletes the vector for the specified document id, so later searches no longer return that id.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_ERASE('idx_fn', 2) AS erase_2;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_REBUILD(index_name)

      Rebuilds one serving index from committed truth rows or the standalone ingest/segment source, depending on consistency mode. The implementation builds a shadow backend outside long locks, then swaps it into service under a short lock.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_REBUILD('idx_fn') AS rebuild_idx_fn;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_BULK_LOAD_BEGIN(index_name)

      Enters bulk-load mode. Later transactional commits update only committed truth rows, while standalone writes update only the ingest/source store; both defer serving-backend updates until bulk build.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_BULK_LOAD_BEGIN('idx_bulk') AS bulk_begin;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_BULK_BUILD(index_name)

      Builds a shadow backend from committed truth rows or the standalone ingest/segment source, depending on consistency mode, and atomically swaps it into service. DiskANN uses diskann_build_mode to choose auto, offline, or serial.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_BULK_BUILD('idx_bulk') AS bulk_build;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_RECOVER(index_name)

      Recovers a transactional index from a recoverable backend artifact or committed truth rows, and a standalone index from its persisted ingest/segment source; it installs the recovered shadow backend on success. If committed/changelog state is corrupt and exactly one table-bound transactional index requires recovery, the function rebuilds the projection from the InnoDB base table under MDL_SHARED_NO_WRITE. Run it as a standalone autocommit statement; use VEC_INDEX_RECOVER_ALL() when multiple indexes require recovery.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_RECOVER('idx_fn') AS recover_idx_fn;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_REBUILD_ALL()

      Rebuilds all registered indexes and returns the number of successfully rebuilt indexes.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_REBUILD_ALL() AS rebuild_all;
    -> 5

      VEC_INDEX_RECOVER_ALL()

      Recovers all registered indexes and returns the number of successfully recovered indexes. For corrupt committed/changelog state, it acquires write-blocking MDL on all bound tables before scanning and atomically publishes the complete projection. This local derived-state repair is not binlogged. Run the function as a standalone autocommit statement.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_RECOVER_ALL() AS recover_all;
    -> 5

      4.3 LOAD VECTOR DATA file import

      LOAD VECTOR DATA is the file-import path for named vector indexes and supports both standalone and transactional run modes. Standalone indexes write the input into private index segments. Transactional indexes publish the input into truth-store committed rows. When REBUILD is specified, the statement also builds the serving backend after import. Without REBUILD, run VEC_INDEX_BULK_BUILD() or VEC_INDEX_REBUILD() explicitly before relying on the latest input for search.

      LOAD VECTOR DATA INFILE 'vectors.csv'
  [DOCID INFILE 'docids.u64']
  INTO VECTOR INDEX index_name
  FORMAT {CSV|FBIN}
  [REPLACE]
  [REBUILD];
      Clause Meaning
      INFILE Server-readable CSV or FBIN file path. The path must ultimately resolve to a regular file; directories, FIFOs, and device files are rejected. Non-LOCAL paths require the FILE privilege and are constrained by secure_file_priv.
      DOCID INFILE Optional docid file for FORMAT FBIN only. Its path must also resolve to a regular file. If omitted, doc ids are generated as 0..rows-1. FORMAT CSV already carries doc ids and cannot use this clause.
      FORMAT CSV Text format with two columns, doc_id,vector. Header rows, blank lines, CRLF line endings, and quoted vector fields such as 201,"[1,0]" are accepted.
      FORMAT FBIN Binary format for high-dimensional bulk import. It can use an optional DOCID INFILE.
      REPLACE Allows duplicate doc ids to replace existing values. Without it, duplicates inside the file or against the current index return an error and keep the old index available.
      REBUILD Rebuilds immediately after import. Without it, the index may remain in bulk_loading or pending-rebuild state and requires an explicit VEC_INDEX_BULK_BUILD() or VEC_INDEX_REBUILD() before the latest input is searchable.

      Privileges and replication: LOAD VECTOR DATA requires the FILE privilege and, for independent named indexes, ALTER on the persisted owner schema. Supported non-LOCAL imports are written to the binlog and replayed on replicas as equivalent vector batch writes; standalone imports with REBUILD also replay one explicit bulk build on the replica. Comparable writes against table-bound indexes check ALTER on the bound table.

      CSV files have two columns: a uint64 doc id and a text vector such as "[1,0]". The FBIN file format is little-endian uint32 rows, uint32 dim, followed by rows * dim little-endian float32 values. The docid file format is little-endian uint64 rows, followed by rows little-endian uint64 doc_id values. This version parses LOCAL syntax, but LOAD VECTOR DATA LOCAL is not yet supported. For client-local files, place the file in a server-readable allowed directory first.

      Example output:

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_CREATE('idx_txn_load_csv', 2, 'EUCLIDEAN', 'MEMORY',
    ->                         'HNSWLIB');
    -> 1

mysql> LOAD VECTOR DATA INFILE 'vector_transactional_load.csv'
    ->   INTO VECTOR INDEX idx_txn_load_csv
    ->   FORMAT CSV
    ->   REBUILD;
Query OK, 2 rows affected

mysql> SELECT JSON_CONTAINS(
    ->   VEC_INDEX_SEARCH('idx_txn_load_csv', VEC_FROMTEXT('[1,0]'), 1),
    ->   CAST(301 AS JSON)) AS csv_doc_visible;
    -> 1

mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_CREATE('idx_load_standalone', 2, 'EUCLIDEAN', 'MEMORY',
    ->                         'HNSWLIB', 0, 'standalone');
    -> 1

mysql> LOAD VECTOR DATA INFILE 'vector_load_data.csv'
    ->   INTO VECTOR INDEX idx_load_standalone
    ->   FORMAT CSV
    ->   REBUILD;
Query OK, 2 rows affected

mysql> SELECT JSON_CONTAINS(
    ->   VEC_INDEX_SEARCH('idx_load_standalone', VEC_FROMTEXT('[0,1]'), 1),
    ->   CAST(202 AS JSON)) AS standalone_csv_visible;
    -> 1

mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_CREATE('idx_load_pending', 2, 'EUCLIDEAN', 'MEMORY',
    ->                         'HNSWLIB', 0, 'standalone');
    -> 1

mysql> LOAD VECTOR DATA INFILE 'vector_load_data.fbin'
    ->   DOCID INFILE 'vector_load_data.u64'
    ->   INTO VECTOR INDEX idx_load_pending
    ->   FORMAT FBIN;
Query OK, 3 rows affected

mysql> SELECT JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx_load_pending'),
    ->                                  '$.lifecycle_state')) AS lifecycle_state;
    -> bulk_loading

mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_BULK_BUILD('idx_load_pending');
    -> 1

mysql> SELECT JSON_UNQUOTE(JSON_EXTRACT(VEC_INDEX_INFO('idx_load_pending'),
    ->                                  '$.build_source')) AS build_source;
    -> raw_segments_direct

      4.4 Backend parameter function summary

      Function Behavior Input parameters Return value
      VEC_INDEX_SET_SEARCH_EF(index_name, ef) Sets HNSW/Faiss HNSW query-time candidate breadth. index_name: index name; ef: query candidate breadth in 1..4294967295. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_SET_HNSW_BUILD_PARAMS(index_name, m, ef_construction) Sets HNSW build parameters. m: maximum graph connections in 1..4294967295; ef_construction: build candidate breadth in 1..4294967295. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_SET_FAISS_IVF_PARAMS(index_name, nlist, nprobe) Switches to or sets Faiss IVF-Flat parameters. nlist: cluster list count in 0..4294967295; nprobe: query-time probed list count in 0..4294967295; both 0 switches back to the Faiss hnsw variant; changing only nprobe does not rebuild the index. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_SET_FAISS_IVFPQ_PARAMS(index_name, nlist, nprobe, pq_m, pq_bits) Switches to or sets Faiss IVF-PQ parameters. nlist, nprobe, and pq_m are in 1..4294967295; pq_m is the PQ subquantizer count and must evenly divide the index vector dimension; pq_bits is the bit count per subquantizer and is in 1..24; changing only nprobe does not rebuild the index. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_BUILD_PARAMS(index_name, max_degree, build_complexity[, build_threads]) Sets DiskANN build parameters. max_degree: maximum graph degree in 1..4294967295; build_complexity: build-time search complexity in 1..4294967295; build_threads: optional build worker count in 0..65535, where 0 or omission uses global vector_diskann_build_threads. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_BUILD_MODE(index_name, mode) Sets the DiskANN build path. mode accepts AUTO, SERIAL, and OFFLINE; the index-level setting overrides global vector_diskann_build_mode; non-DiskANN indexes accept AUTO as a no-op only, and other modes return 0 with a warning; non-DiskANN indexes report the default auto in VEC_INDEX_INFO(). 1 on success; query the current value through the diskann_build_mode field in VEC_INDEX_INFO().
      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_COMPLEXITY(index_name, search_complexity) Sets DiskANN query complexity. search_complexity: query-time search complexity in 1..4294967295. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_BEAMWIDTH(index_name, beamwidth) Sets DiskANN offline-adapter query beam width. beamwidth: query-time candidate beam width in 1..128. 1 on success; query the current value through the diskann_search_beamwidth field in VEC_INDEX_INFO().
      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_PQ_CODE_BUDGET_SIZE(index_name, size) Sets the DiskANN offline-build PQ code budget. size: byte budget; 0..18446744073709551615 on 64-bit platforms and 0..4294967295 on 32-bit platforms; 0 derives the budget from vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_ratio. 1 on success; query the current value through the diskann_pq_code_budget_size field in VEC_INDEX_INFO().
      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_DISK_PQ_DIMS(index_name, dims) Sets the PQ dimensions used by the DiskANN disk layout. dims is in 0..512; 0 selects automatic sizing. Non-DiskANN indexes accept only 0 as a no-op. 1 on success; query the current value through diskann_disk_pq_dims in VEC_INDEX_INFO().
      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_ACCELERATE_BUILD(index_name, enabled) Sets the DiskANN offline accelerated-build hint. enabled accepts only 0 or 1. Non-DiskANN indexes accept only 0 as a no-op. 1 on success; query the current value through diskann_accelerate_build in VEC_INDEX_INFO().
      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SHUFFLE_BUILD(index_name, enabled) Sets the DiskANN offline input-batch shuffle hint. enabled accepts only 0 or 1. Non-DiskANN indexes accept only 0 as a no-op. 1 on success; query the current value through diskann_shuffle_build in VEC_INDEX_INFO().
      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_USE_BFS_CACHE(index_name, enabled) Sets the BFS-cache hint for DiskANN offline load. enabled accepts only 0 or 1. Non-DiskANN indexes accept only 0 as a no-op. 1 on success; query the current value through diskann_use_bfs_cache in VEC_INDEX_INFO().

      4.5 Backend parameter function details and examples

      Backend parameter functions update index configuration. Parameters that affect build-time behavior usually require an explicit VEC_INDEX_REBUILD() or VEC_INDEX_REBUILD_ALL() before an existing serving index uses the new values.

      VEC_INDEX_SET_SEARCH_EF(index_name, ef)

      Sets HNSW/Faiss HNSW query-time candidate breadth. Larger values usually improve recall at higher query cost.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_SEARCH_EF('idx_hnsw', 96) AS set_search_ef;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_HNSW_BUILD_PARAMS(index_name, m, ef_construction)

      Sets HNSW graph connection and construction candidate breadth. These values affect later bulk build / rebuild graph quality and build cost.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_HNSW_BUILD_PARAMS('idx_hnsw', 24, 320) AS set_hnsw_build;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_FAISS_IVF_PARAMS(index_name, nlist, nprobe)

      Switches to the Faiss IVF-Flat variant and sets list count and probed-list count. nlist=0,nprobe=0 switches back to the Faiss hnsw variant. nlist is a build-time parameter and changing it regenerates the sidecar; changing only nprobe with the same nlist is query-time tuning and does not rebuild the index.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_FAISS_IVF_PARAMS('idx_faiss', 2, 1) AS set_faiss_ivf;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_FAISS_IVFPQ_PARAMS(index_name, nlist, nprobe, pq_m, pq_bits)

      Switches to the Faiss IVF-PQ variant. nlist, pq_m, and pq_bits are build-time parameters and changing them regenerates the sidecar; changing only nprobe is query-time tuning. pq_m is the number of PQ subquantizers, and pq_bits is the bit count per subquantizer. pq_m must evenly divide the index vector dimension, and pq_bits must be in 1..24. Configurations outside these constraints are rejected before a durable publication intent is written.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_FAISS_IVFPQ_PARAMS('idx_ivfpq', 2, 1, 1, 1) AS set_faiss_ivfpq;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_BUILD_PARAMS(index_name, max_degree, build_complexity[, build_threads])

      Sets DiskANN graph degree, build complexity, and optional build worker count. Omitting build_threads or passing 0 uses global vector_diskann_build_threads.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_BUILD_PARAMS('idx_diskann', 48, 96, 4) AS set_diskann_build;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_BUILD_MODE(index_name, mode)

      Sets the DiskANN index-level build path, which takes precedence over global vector_diskann_build_mode. Non-DiskANN indexes accept only AUTO as a no-op; other values return 0 and emit a warning.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_BUILD_MODE('idx_diskann', 'OFFLINE') AS set_diskann_mode;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_COMPLEXITY(index_name, search_complexity)

      Sets DiskANN query complexity, meaning the underlying search-list size. Larger values usually improve recall at higher CPU and IO cost. During exact rerank and segmented serving, MySQL-Vector uses a backend candidate window smaller than the search list, preserves DiskANN's returned_candidates < search_list search semantics, and reranks with exact vectors before returning the final SQL topK. Multi-segment DiskANN search divides search_complexity - 1 by the fan-out segment count for each segment candidate window, so the per-segment window is not compressed too aggressively.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_COMPLEXITY('idx_diskann', 80) AS set_diskann_search;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_BEAMWIDTH(index_name, beamwidth)

      Sets DiskANN offline-adapter query beam width. The value maps to the candidate beam expanded by the underlying DiskANN query loop; the default 16 is a conservative multi-core parallelism cap. The valid range is 1..128; to improve recall, increase search_complexity first and do not set beamwidth to search-list values such as 800 or 1200.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_BEAMWIDTH('idx_diskann', 16) AS set_diskann_beamwidth;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_PQ_CODE_BUDGET_SIZE(index_name, size)

      Sets the total PQ code budget for DiskANN offline build. It is a build-time parameter, not a query-time tuning knob. After changing it on an existing index, run VEC_INDEX_REBUILD() explicitly before expecting the serving index to use the new value; setting the same value again is a no-op. 0 is the recommended default and derives the budget from vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_ratio. To pin an explicit budget, estimate row_count * dimension * 4 * ratio; the default ratio is 0.125. For example, a 1024D float vector is 4096 raw bytes per row, so the default ratio derives an approximately 512 bytes/vector PQ-code budget before converting it to DiskANN num_of_pq_chunks with floor(total_budget / row_count).

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_PQ_CODE_BUDGET_SIZE('idx_diskann', 0) AS set_diskann_pq_budget;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_DISK_PQ_DIMS(index_name, dims)

      Sets the disk-PQ dimensions used by the DiskANN disk layout. 0 uses recall-first automatic selection, while explicit values are in 1..512. This is a build-time setting, so rebuild the index before expecting an existing serving artifact to change.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_DISK_PQ_DIMS('idx_diskann', 2) AS set_diskann_pq_dims;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_ACCELERATE_BUILD(index_name, enabled)

      Allows a capable DiskANN offline adapter to use accelerated build paths. The value must be 0 or 1; backends without this hint keep the standard build path.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_ACCELERATE_BUILD('idx_diskann', 1) AS set_diskann_accelerate;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SHUFFLE_BUILD(index_name, enabled)

      Allows a capable DiskANN offline adapter to shuffle build input batches. The value must be 0 or 1.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SHUFFLE_BUILD('idx_diskann', 1) AS set_diskann_shuffle;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_USE_BFS_CACHE(index_name, enabled)

      Controls whether DiskANN offline load is forced to use the BFS cache. The value must be 0 or 1. Even with 0, BFS cache is selected automatically when search cache nodes are configured.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_USE_BFS_CACHE('idx_diskann', 1) AS set_diskann_bfs_cache;
    -> 1

      Special notes:

      • VEC_INDEX_SET_FAISS_IVF_PARAMS(index_name, 0, 0) switches back to the Faiss hnsw variant.
      • Build-time parameters are saved in index configuration. To make build-time parameters take effect immediately, run VEC_INDEX_REBUILD(...) or VEC_INDEX_REBUILD_ALL() explicitly.
      • Faiss build worker count currently applies only when creating an index, bulk building, rebuilding, and bulk-loading an in-memory backend. It does not change query parallelism; batch query execution is scheduled by Faiss Index::search(nq, ...) itself.
      • DiskANN native build / rebuild does not rewrite the persistent native store directly during construction. The persistent directory is replaced only after both native build and memory-store flush succeed. On failure, the old serving backend and old native store remain available.
      • After DiskANN native build / rebuild succeeds, the current mysqld process keeps a resident native store to serve queries and preferentially answers DiskANN callbacks from that resident store. The persistent directory is used for restart recovery or resident-miss fallback. This reduces per-key file open overhead during query execution, but increases process memory usage according to native-store size.
      • DiskANN AUTO prefers the MySQL-Vector vendored runtime/offline adapter and falls back to official serial insertion when offline is unavailable or the input is too small for the minimum graph; other offline build failures do not automatically downgrade. SERIAL forces the official serial Garnet ABI, and OFFLINE uses only the vendored runtime/offline adapter. When the adapter is not configured or cannot be loaded, explicit OFFLINE rebuild fails instead of falling back to serial insertion.
      • The DiskANN offline adapter calls only existing official DiskANN C++ interfaces and does not require DiskANN source changes. When LOAD VECTOR DATA or standalone raw ingest produces one valid raw FBIN segment, the adapter passes that file directly to DiskANN build. Multiple raw segments build one DiskANN serving handle per segment by default, and search fans out to all segments before globally merging topK. Set vector_diskann_segmented_serving=OFF only to merge raw segments into one logical manifest, build it once through DiskANN offline build, and load one serving handle for diagnostics. Use backend_build_segments to see the input segment count and backend_build_invocations to confirm how many backend builds were actually executed.
      • Table-bound transactional DiskANN offline CREATE VECTOR INDEX persists backfilled rows into the InnoDB hidden truth-store. DROP VECTOR INDEX first persists the deleted metadata/manifest state, then cleans hidden committed rows. Large backfill/drop operations need an innodb_redo_log_capacity sized for the data volume; do not treat MTR's default 10MB redo capacity as a production configuration during load tests.
      • Runtime tuning persists only index metadata and manifest checkpoints. It should not rewrite committed vector data, the change log, or prepared transaction records. The implementation uses a two-phase model: build candidate backend/config first, persist metadata/manifest, and switch the live configuration only after persistence succeeds. If persistence fails, the old configuration stays effective and an error is returned.

      4.6 DiskANN build mode

      diskann_build_mode has operational meaning only for DiskANN indexes. HNSWLIB, Faiss, Native, and other non-DiskANN indexes return auto in VEC_INDEX_INFO() only as the unified field's default placeholder; it does not affect those backends.

      vector_diskann_build_mode is the global default. It affects only DiskANN indexes without an explicit index-level mode. The index-level VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_BUILD_MODE(index_name, mode) value takes precedence over the global variable. Non-DiskANN indexes accept auto as a no-op only; other modes return 0 and emit a warning. Indexes without an explicit index-level mode use the current global value on later rebuilds.

      Mode Meaning Recommended use
      auto Prefers the MySQL-Vector vendored runtime/offline adapter; if offline support is unavailable or the input is too small for the minimum graph, falls back to the official serial ABI. Other offline build failures do not automatically downgrade. Default recommendation for production and tests that should not depend on a specific DiskANN build ABI.
      serial Forces the official Garnet serial insert path. Use when compatibility matters most or when isolating offline-adapter issues.
      offline Uses only the MySQL-Vector vendored runtime/offline adapter to call the official DiskANN offline build path and fails explicitly when the adapter is missing or cannot be loaded. Prefer for large DiskANN imports or rebuilds, especially when a failed build must not disturb the old serving index.

      4.7 Build/search path and fallback flow

      HNSWLIB, FAISS, and DiskANN standalone indexes use the MySQL-Vector native runtime. If the matching direct backend was not compiled or its runtime library is unavailable, create or rebuild fails; these indexes do not automatically fall back to transactional backends.

      Build / rebuild path

      VEC_INDEX_REBUILD / VEC_INDEX_BULK_BUILD / CREATE VECTOR INDEX backfill
  |
  +-- transactional
  |     |
  |     +-- read committed truth-store rows
  |     |
  |     +-- HNSWLIB + MEMORY
  |     |     +-- provider not compiled: creation/recovery fails at factory
  |     |     +-- compiled provider: hnswlib parallel graph rebuild
  |     |     +-- compiled provider with unavailable native state: internal exact fallback
  |     |
  |     +-- FAISS + MEMORY/EXTERNAL
  |     |     +-- provider not compiled: creation/recovery fails at factory
  |     |     +-- compiled provider: Faiss build/rebuild
  |     |     +-- external mode: persist snapshot for supported recovery fallback
  |     |
  |     +-- DiskANN + EXTERNAL
  |     |     +-- auto: vendored runtime/offline adapter when available, otherwise official serial insert
  |     |     +-- serial: official serial insert
  |     |     +-- offline: vendored runtime/offline adapter only; missing adapter fails
  |     |
  |     +-- NATIVE: memory/external exact backend
  |     |
  |     +-- success: atomic swap to serving backend
  |     +-- failure: keep the previous serving backend and report lifecycle failure
  |
  +-- standalone
        |
        +-- read standalone ingest cache / raw segment manifest
        |
        +-- HNSWLIB / FAISS / DiskANN
        |     +-- MySQL-Vector native runtime invokes the matching direct backend
        |     +-- no matching direct backend/runtime: fail; no transactional fallback
        |
        +-- NATIVE: memory/external exact backend
        |
        +-- direct upsert/erase: mark bulk_loading
        +-- explicit rebuild success: mark ready and reopen search
        +-- rebuild failure before publish:
              restore the previous lifecycle; keep a first build bulk_loading,
              or continue serving the previous ready generation

      Search path

      VEC_INDEX_SEARCH / VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_BATCH
  |
  +-- lifecycle == bulk_loading / creating / backfilling
  |     +-- reject search until rebuild/backfill succeeds
  |
  +-- ensure runtime is loaded
  |     +-- lazy external runtime can rebuild from source before search
  |
  +-- transactional runtime
  |     |
  |     +-- HNSWLIB: native hnswlib search; a built internal exact fallback may serve when native state is unavailable
  |     +-- FAISS: Faiss search/batch search; external snapshot fallback is limited to supported recovery/search failures
  |     +-- DiskANN: loaded vendored/offline/segmented/serial runtime search;
  |     |            external snapshot fallback only for supported auto-recovery cases
  |     +-- HNSWLIB MEMORY search failure without pending changes:
  |           rebuild from truth-store once and retry
  |
  +-- standalone runtime
        |
        +-- HNSWLIB/FAISS/DiskANN: MySQL-Vector native runtime direct backend search/batch search
        +-- NATIVE: memory/external exact backend
        +-- no matching direct backend/runtime: no runtime exists; creation failed earlier

      5. Explicit transaction buffer functions

      Explicit transaction functions are used to commit or roll back multiple vector writes as a group. For single direct writes, prefer VEC_INDEX_UPSERT() / VEC_INDEX_ERASE().

      Normal SQL transactions for real table-mapped indexes participate in the MySQL savepoint protocol through the hidden handlerton. SAVEPOINT name, ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT name, and RELEASE SAVEPOINT name trim or release staged vector changes together with the SQL transaction. Explicit VEC_INDEX_TXN_* savepoint functions are only for directly managing standalone vector transaction buffers.

      A transaction id created by VEC_INDEX_TXN_BEGIN() belongs only to the creating connection and authenticated account. Another connection cannot inspect, stage, commit, or roll back it even when using the same account. Normal disconnect, KILL, or COM_CHANGE_USER automatically rolls back every unfinished explicit vector transaction owned by that connection.

      5.1 Explicit transaction function summary

      Function Behavior Input parameters Return value
      VEC_INDEX_TXN_BEGIN() Starts an explicit vector transaction. None. Unsigned 64-bit transaction id allocated inside the current instance.
      VEC_INDEX_STAGE_UPSERT(index_name, txn_id, doc_id, vector_expr) Stages an upsert in a vector transaction. index_name: index name; txn_id: unsigned 64-bit transaction id; doc_id: unsigned 64-bit document id; vector_expr: vector value. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_STAGE_ERASE(index_name, txn_id, doc_id) Stages an erase in a vector transaction. index_name: index name; txn_id: unsigned 64-bit transaction id; doc_id: unsigned 64-bit document id. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_TXN_PENDING(txn_id) Returns the number of staged changes in a transaction buffer. txn_id: unsigned 64-bit transaction id. Pending upsert / erase count.
      VEC_INDEX_TXN_SAVEPOINT(txn_id, name) Creates a vector transaction savepoint. txn_id: unsigned 64-bit transaction id; name: savepoint name. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_TXN_ROLLBACK_TO(txn_id, name) Rolls back staged vector changes to the named savepoint. txn_id: unsigned 64-bit transaction id; name: savepoint name. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_TXN_RELEASE_SAVEPOINT(txn_id, name) Releases a vector transaction savepoint. txn_id: unsigned 64-bit transaction id; name: savepoint name. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_TXN_COMMIT(txn_id) Commits staged changes. txn_id: unsigned 64-bit transaction id. 1 on success.
      VEC_INDEX_TXN_ROLLBACK(txn_id) Rolls back staged changes. txn_id: unsigned 64-bit transaction id. 1 on success.

      5.2 Explicit transaction function details and examples

      txn_id is allocated inside the current instance, so the numeric value changes with execution history. The value 1 below is an actual sample from one run.

      VEC_INDEX_TXN_BEGIN()

      Starts an explicit vector transaction buffer and returns the transaction id allocated by the current instance.

      mysql> SET @txn = VEC_INDEX_TXN_BEGIN();
mysql> SELECT @txn AS txn_id;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_STAGE_UPSERT(index_name, txn_id, doc_id, vector_expr)

      Stages an upsert in the specified vector transaction. The staged write is not visible to normal search until commit.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_STAGE_UPSERT('idx_txn', @txn, 10, VEC_FROMTEXT('[1,1]')) AS stage_upsert_10;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_TXN_PENDING(txn_id)

      Returns the number of staged changes that have not yet been committed or rolled back.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_TXN_PENDING(@txn) AS pending_after_one;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_TXN_SAVEPOINT(txn_id, name)

      Creates a named savepoint inside an explicit vector transaction so later changes can be rolled back to that point.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_TXN_SAVEPOINT(@txn, 'sp1') AS savepoint_sp1;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_TXN_ROLLBACK_TO(txn_id, name)

      Rolls back staged vector changes created after the named savepoint.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_TXN_ROLLBACK_TO(@txn, 'sp1') AS rollback_to_sp1;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_TXN_RELEASE_SAVEPOINT(txn_id, name)

      Releases a named savepoint without committing or rolling back staged changes.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_TXN_RELEASE_SAVEPOINT(@txn, 'sp1') AS release_sp1;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_TXN_COMMIT(txn_id)

      Commits all staged vector changes into committed truth rows and updates serving state according to index lifecycle.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_TXN_COMMIT(@txn) AS commit_txn;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_STAGE_ERASE(index_name, txn_id, doc_id)

      Stages an erase in the specified vector transaction. After commit, the document id is removed.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_STAGE_ERASE('idx_txn', @txn2, 10) AS stage_erase_10;
    -> 1

      VEC_INDEX_TXN_ROLLBACK(txn_id)

      Discards all staged changes in the transaction buffer without affecting already committed vector data.

      mysql> SELECT VEC_INDEX_TXN_ROLLBACK(@txn2) AS rollback_txn2;
    -> 1

      6. Table-level DDL

      6.1 Syntax

      CREATE VECTOR INDEX ON tbl(col) WITH (dim)
CREATE VECTOR INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ON tbl(col) WITH (dim)
CREATE VECTOR INDEX ON tbl(col) WITH (dim, metric, mode, provider)
CREATE VECTOR INDEX ON tbl(col) WITH (dim, metric, mode, provider, consistency_mode)
CREATE VECTOR INDEX ON tbl(col) WITH (dim, metric, mode, provider, build_threads)
CREATE VECTOR INDEX ON tbl(col) WITH (dim, metric, mode, provider, build_threads, consistency_mode)

DROP VECTOR INDEX ON tbl(col)
DROP VECTOR INDEX IF EXISTS ON tbl(col)

      WITH parameter meanings:

      • dim: vector dimension in 1..1073741823; it must match the target VECTOR(dim) column.
      • metric: supports EUCLIDEAN, L2, COSINE, IP, and INNER_PRODUCT.
      • mode: supports MEMORY and EXTERNAL.
      • provider: supports NATIVE, FAISS, DISKANN, HNSWLIB, and HNSW.
      • build_threads: only applies to provider='HNSWLIB', provider='FAISS', or provider='DISKANN'. The range is 0..65535. A statement-level value greater than 0 overrides the corresponding global variable vector_hnsw_build_threads, vector_faiss_build_threads, or vector_diskann_build_threads; statement-level 0 means inherit the global setting, and global 0 means automatic.
      • consistency_mode: table-bound indexes accept only transactional; explicit standalone returns an error. The parameter may follow the provider directly or follow build_threads.

      Privileges follow MySQL single-table index DDL semantics: creating or dropping a table-bound vector index requires INDEX on the target table; tuning, rebuilding, explicitly writing to, or recovering that bound index requires ALTER.

      6.2 VEC_INDEX_CREATE vs CREATE VECTOR INDEX

      Area VEC_INDEX_CREATE() CREATE VECTOR INDEX
      Index name Caller-supplied independent name, such as idx_fn. Derived as schema.table.column, such as readme_vector.t_ddl.v.
      Table binding Not bound to a table column; consistency may be transactional or standalone. Bound to one InnoDB VECTOR(dim) column and always transactional.
      Data source Explicit VEC_INDEX_UPSERT(), VEC_INDEX_ERASE(), batch API, or file import. Transactional mode uses truth rows; standalone mode uses its private ingest/segment source. Backfills existing rows at creation and then synchronizes normal table DML automatically.
      Privilege check Checks CREATE on the owner schema at creation, then checks ALTER / DROP / SELECT on the persisted owner. Checks INDEX on the affected table for create or drop, and ALTER for tuning, rebuild, explicit writes, or recovery.
      Metadata export Does not appear in SHOW CREATE TABLE. SHOW CREATE TABLE emits the CREATE VECTOR INDEX definition.
      Typical use Smoke tests, benchmarks, explicit lifecycle control. Application tables and production-style usage.

      Independent named-index example:

      SELECT VEC_INDEX_CREATE('idx_fn', 2, 'EUCLIDEAN', 'MEMORY', 'HNSWLIB') AS create_idx_fn;
+---------------+
| create_idx_fn |
+---------------+
|             1 |
+---------------+

SELECT VEC_INDEX_LIST() AS index_list_after_create;
+-------------------------+
| index_list_after_create |
+-------------------------+
| ["idx_fn"]              |
+-------------------------+

      Table-level vector indexes bind an InnoDB table column to a vector serving index. The index name is derived from schema, table, and column metadata rather than supplied in the SQL statement. DML updates are propagated through the vector transaction bridge and recover from truth rows.

      CREATE TABLE t_ddl (
  id BIGINT PRIMARY KEY,
  v VECTOR(2)
) ENGINE=InnoDB;

CREATE VECTOR INDEX ON t_ddl(v)
WITH (2, 'euclidean', 'memory', 'hnswlib');

SHOW CREATE TABLE t_ddl\G
*************************** 1. row ***************************
       Table: t_ddl
Create Table: CREATE TABLE `t_ddl` (
  `id` bigint NOT NULL,
  `v` vector(2) DEFAULT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (`id`)
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci
CREATE VECTOR INDEX ON `t_ddl`(`v`) WITH (2, 'euclidean', 'memory', 'hnswlib')

      6.3 Usage limits

      • The target column must be a VECTOR(dim) column, and WITH (dim) must match the column dimension.
      • CREATE VECTOR INDEX currently supports only InnoDB base tables; non-InnoDB tables are rejected.
      • Mapped indexes currently require a single-column, non-null integer primary key as doc_id. Unsigned integer primary keys support the full uint64_t range; negative signed integer primary-key values are not valid doc_id values.
      • CREATE VECTOR INDEX / DROP VECTOR INDEX is not supported on temporary tables.
      • CREATE VECTOR INDEX holds a no-write metadata lock during backfill to avoid having the scan snapshot overwrite vector changes committed by concurrent DML.
      • CREATE VECTOR INDEX internally uses the creating -> backfilling -> ready lifecycle. If mysqld restarts before ready, recovery cleans up metadata, committed entries, change log, and prepared rows for the incomplete index, and it does not expose a half-built index to queries.
      • Table open revalidates the persisted VECTOR DD representation. is_vector and vector_dim must appear together, the dimension must be valid, the physical field must be a capacity-compatible binary BLOB, and the stored length must not be shorter than the vector payload. Any mismatch fails table open with ER_INVALID_DD_OBJECT instead of silently treating a damaged vector column as an ordinary BLOB. Restore a consistent data dictionary or valid backup before retrying.

      6.4 Lifecycle coupling

      The vector index lifecycle follows these table-level operations:

      • CREATE TABLE ... LIKE
      • RENAME TABLE
      • ALTER TABLE ... RENAME TO
      • ALTER TABLE ... RENAME COLUMN
      • ALTER TABLE ... CHANGE/MODIFY COLUMN
      • ALTER TABLE ... DROP COLUMN
      • TRUNCATE TABLE
      • DROP TABLE
      • DROP DATABASE

      7. Status inspection

      7.1 INFORMATION_SCHEMA.VECTOR_LIBRARIES

      INFORMATION_SCHEMA.VECTOR_LIBRARIES shows the optional vector libraries compiled into the current binary, similar to how INFORMATION_SCHEMA.ENGINES shows storage-engine capabilities. It always lists three rows: faiss, diskann, and hnsw. SUPPORT='YES' means the current build includes that library; SUPPORT='NO' means the library was not compiled, is unavailable on the platform, or was disabled by the matching MYSQL_VECTOR_* master switch. The built-in native provider is not an optional third-party library, so it is not listed. OFFLINE_BUILD reports whether an offline-build entry point was integrated at compile time: DiskANN reports YES or NO, while FAISS and HNSW currently report N/A. The column does not probe the filesystem at query time. If it reports YES but the configured dynamic adapter is missing, corrupt, or has unloadable dependencies, the build still fails rather than silently falling back or changing the value to NO. The native provider is an exact backend only for MySQL debug builds where NDEBUG is not defined and at least one real vector library is compiled. It is intended for tests and debugging, not for production indexing or as a fallback when third-party libraries are absent.

      A Linux Debug build with all backends enabled produced this output. SUPPORT and OFFLINE_BUILD values can differ between builds:

      mysql> SELECT LIBRARY, SUPPORT, OFFLINE_BUILD, COMMENT
    -> FROM INFORMATION_SCHEMA.VECTOR_LIBRARIES
    -> ORDER BY LIBRARY;
+---------+---------+---------------+--------------------------------+
| LIBRARY | SUPPORT | OFFLINE_BUILD | COMMENT                        |
+---------+---------+---------------+--------------------------------+
| diskann | YES     | YES           | DiskANN backend is compiled in |
| faiss   | YES     | N/A           | Faiss backend is compiled in   |
| hnsw    | YES     | N/A           | hnswlib backend is compiled in |
+---------+---------+---------------+--------------------------------+

      If a library reports NO, creating an index with that provider fails. If all three libraries report NO, the binary still supports the VECTOR(dim) type and scalar vector functions, but it cannot create searchable vector indexes. To enable that capability, configure and rebuild MySQL, for example with -DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN=ON and the corresponding DiskANN path options. The vector_default_library setting only affects provider-omitted creation; when it is empty, omitting provider fails. Setting it to a library with SUPPORT='NO' raises an error during startup or runtime assignment.

      7.2 SHOW VECTOR STATUS

      Supported forms:

      SHOW VECTOR STATUS;
SHOW VECTOR STATUS LIKE 'pattern';
SHOW VECTOR STATUS WHERE expr;
SHOW VECTOR STATUS FOR INDEX index_name;
SHOW VECTOR STATUS FOR INDEX index_name LIKE 'pattern';
SHOW VECTOR STATUS FOR INDEX index_name WHERE expr;

      Notes:

      • Requires the global PROCESS privilege.
      • Use this interface for automated collection.
      • WHERE currently supports =, !=, <>, LIKE, and NOT LIKE on Section, Variable_name, and Value, plus >, >=, <, and <= comparisons on Value. Predicates can be combined with AND / OR. IN (...) is not supported.

      Section meanings:

      Section Meaning
      GLOBAL_COUNTERS Global request, failure, and rollback counters.
      GLOBAL_GAUGES Current registered-index count, manifest checkpoints, truth-store state, and committed/pending vector-memory gauges.
      INDEX_STATE Per-index configuration, table-column binding, lifecycle, and entry counts.
      BACKEND_HEALTH Whether a per-index backend is loaded and writable, plus error code and external manifest state.
      SYNC_PIPELINE Per-index pending changes, latest apply latency, and rebuild/recover progress.

      7.3 Status field meanings

      GLOBAL_GAUGES memory fields:

      Field name Meaning
      Vector_committed_vector_memory_bytes Payload bytes currently held by the SQL-layer committed vector cache; constrained by vector_entry_cache_size.
      Vector_pending_vector_memory_bytes Vector payload bytes still held in memory by all uncommitted transactions; payloads already written to spill files are not included.

      Global backend, synchronization, and incremental-persistence status:

      SectionField nameMeaning
      GLOBAL_COUNTERSVector_truth_store_delta_persist_requestsRequests through the truth-store incremental-persist path.
      GLOBAL_COUNTERSVector_truth_store_delta_persist_failuresFailed truth-store incremental-persist operations.
      GLOBAL_COUNTERSVector_truth_store_compact_requestsTruth-store change-log compaction requests.
      GLOBAL_COUNTERSVector_truth_store_compact_failuresFailed truth-store change-log compactions.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_loaded_indexesIndexes whose serving backend is currently loaded.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_writable_indexesLoaded indexes whose backend supports mutations.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_readonly_indexesIndexes that do not currently satisfy the writable predicate.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_error_indexesIndexes with a nonzero last_error_code.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_manifest_present_indexesIndexes with an external sidecar manifest.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_manifest_generation_maxLargest external manifest generation across all indexes.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_mode_memory_indexesIndexes in memory mode.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_mode_external_indexesIndexes in external mode.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_provider_native_indexesIndexes using the native provider.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_provider_faiss_indexesIndexes using the faiss provider.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_provider_diskann_indexesIndexes using the diskann provider.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_provider_hnswlib_indexesIndexes using the hnswlib provider.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_lifecycle_ready_indexesIndexes in the ready lifecycle state.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_lifecycle_rebuilding_indexesIndexes in the rebuilding lifecycle state.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_lifecycle_recovering_indexesIndexes in the recovering lifecycle state.
      BACKEND_HEALTHVector_backend_lifecycle_failed_indexesIndexes in the failed lifecycle state.
      SYNC_PIPELINEVector_pending_apply_countSum of pending applies across all indexes.
      SYNC_PIPELINEVector_pending_apply_totalCompatibility alias for Vector_pending_apply_count; the values are identical.
      SYNC_PIPELINEVector_rebuild_progressInteger average of rebuild progress across registered indexes; 0 when no index exists.
      SYNC_PIPELINEVector_recover_progressInteger average of recovery progress across registered indexes; 0 when no index exists.
      SYNC_PIPELINEVector_backlog_indexesIndexes whose pending_apply_count is greater than 0.

      INDEX_STATE fields include dimension, metric, mode, provider, consistency mode, truth-store enablement, build source, build pipeline decision fields, standalone ingest memory bytes, standalone segment count/bytes, standalone raw segment count/bytes, backend variant, owner schema, table binding names, HNSW/Faiss/DiskANN tuning parameters, lifecycle state/version, mutation support, serving entry count, and committed entry count. For an independent named index, owner_schema is the persisted authorization schema. For a table-bound index, it reports the bound table schema for metadata completeness, while authorization still checks the bound table. A value of 0 for backend-specific tuning fields means the backend does not use that parameter or automatic/default behavior is active. consistency_mode is transactional or standalone; truth_store_enabled tells whether the authoritative source is the truth-store; build_source is truth_store, memory, raw_segments_direct, raw_segments_compacted, or delta_replay. committed_entry_count is the authoritative-source row count: it comes from the truth-store committed snapshot for transactional indexes, and from the current standalone ingest/source set for standalone indexes. Pipeline fields describe the latest build decision: build_pipeline_mode is auto, direct, or segmented; build_pipeline_decision is direct or segmented; build_pipeline_trigger is forced_direct, forced_segmented, raw_segment_count, row_count, payload_size, or below_threshold; build_pipeline_rows, build_pipeline_payload_size, and build_pipeline_raw_segments preserve the input statistics used for that decision. Segment policy fields expose the runtime segmentation policy used for the latest decision: build_segment_profile is manual, throughput, balanced, or high_recall; build_segment_profile_reason explains whether the profile was applied, skipped, or forced off; build_segment_effective_target_size is the actual byte target after applying the profile; build_segment_effective_row_limit is the derived row cap; build_segment_target_size and build_segment_max_rows mirror the current global variables, and build_segment_policy is size_first when the effective byte target controls the cap or row_limited when the explicit row cap controls it. standalone_raw_segment_count and standalone_raw_segment_bytes count raw FBIN/docid files imported by LOAD VECTOR DATA; transactional indexes report 0. DiskANN backend_variant reports the actual runtime path, such as diskann_vendored_offline, diskann_offline, diskann_serial, diskann_fallback, diskann_empty, diskann_unloaded, or segmented. diskann_empty means that a zero-row index was published as a recoverable empty generation; search returns no rows, and a later write or rebuild can produce the actual index. With standalone raw-only DiskANN, multiple raw segments default to segmented serving, so the outer backend reports backend_variant=segmented, backend_build_invocations follows the raw segment count, and search fans out to all segments. When vector_diskann_segmented_serving=OFF, the diagnostic fallback merges raw segments into one logical offline build, so backend_build_segments can be greater than backend_build_invocations.

      INDEX_STATE fields:

      Field suffixMeaning
      dimensionVector dimension configured for the index.
      metricDistance metric: euclidean, cosine, or inner_product.
      modeServing mode: memory or external.
      providerServing provider: native, faiss, diskann, or hnswlib.
      schema_nameSchema bound by table-level DDL; empty for an independent named index.
      table_nameTable bound by table-level DDL; empty for an independent named index.
      column_nameVector column bound by table-level DDL; empty for an independent named index.
      search_efHNSW/Faiss-HNSW query candidate breadth; 0 when not used.
      hnsw_mHNSW maximum graph connection count.
      hnsw_ef_constructionHNSW construction candidate breadth.
      hnsw_build_threadsIndex-level hnswlib build worker setting; 0 inherits the global setting.
      faiss_nlistFaiss IVF list count; 0 selects the HNSW variant.
      faiss_nprobeFaiss IVF query-time probed-list count.
      faiss_pq_mFaiss IVF-PQ subquantizer count.
      faiss_pq_bitsBits per Faiss IVF-PQ subquantizer.
      faiss_build_threadsIndex-level Faiss build worker setting; 0 inherits the global setting.
      diskann_max_degreeDiskANN graph maximum degree.
      diskann_build_complexityDiskANN build-time search complexity.
      diskann_build_threadsIndex-level DiskANN build worker setting; 0 inherits the global setting.
      diskann_build_modeDiskANN build mode: auto, serial, or offline.
      diskann_search_beamwidthDiskANN offline search beam width; 0 when not used.
      standalone_ingest_memory_bytesStandalone payload bytes still held in the ingest cache; transactional indexes report 0.
      standalone_segment_countPersisted standalone segment count; transactional indexes report 0.
      standalone_segment_bytesTotal bytes of persisted standalone segments; transactional indexes report 0.
      lifecycle_stateCurrent index lifecycle state.
      lifecycle_versionMonotonic lifecycle-state version.
      supports_mutations1 when the current backend accepts live mutations.
      diskann_pq_code_budget_sizeIndex-level total PQ-code budget for DiskANN offline build; 0 derives it from the global ratio.
      diskann_disk_pq_dimsIndex-level disk-PQ dimensions for the DiskANN disk layout; 0 selects automatic sizing.
      diskann_cache_nodesCache-node count configured for DiskANN offline load; 0 means no explicit count.
      diskann_accelerate_build1 enables the accelerated-build hint for this index when the adapter supports it.
      diskann_shuffle_build1 enables the build-input shuffle hint for this index when the adapter supports it.
      diskann_use_bfs_cache1 forces BFS cache for this index; configured cache nodes can still select BFS cache automatically.
      last_apply_latency_msTime spent by the latest vector change apply, in milliseconds; it has the same value as SYNC_PIPELINE.apply_latency_ms for this index.
      index_identityDurable identity allocated at index creation. Drop and same-name recreate do not reuse it, preventing stale-publication ABA replay.
      truth_generationLogical generation committed to the index's authoritative input source.
      config_generationGeneration of the index configuration that has been persisted and installed.
      artifact_generationTruth generation represented by the current recoverable backend artifact.
      runtime_generationTruth generation represented by the published serving runtime. If it trails truth_generation, rebuild or recovery must catch up before ANN service continues.

      BACKEND_HEALTH fields include backend type, variant, mode, loaded/writable flags, the latest lifecycle error code and timestamp, recovery fallback counters, build diagnostics, scheduler diagnostics, DiskANN segment diagnostics, and external sidecar manifest presence/generation. Build diagnostics are emitted only after a build/rebuild path records them; they include backend_build_runtime (vendored_runtime, official_cpp_main, serial, or serial_fallback), backend_build_input_source, backend_build_rows, backend_build_segments, backend_build_invocations, backend_build_pq_chunks, backend_build_cache_nodes, backend_build_manifest_ms, backend_build_offline_ms, backend_build_load_ms, diskann_pq_runtime, native_pq_runtime_selected_path, native_pq_runtime_elapsed_ms, native_pq_runtime_train_ms, native_pq_runtime_encode_ms, native_pq_runtime_artifact_validation_ms, native_pq_runtime_centroid_scan_kernel, native_pq_runtime_distance_calls, native_pq_runtime_effective_threads, native_pq_runtime_encode_block_rows, native_pq_runtime_memory_estimate, native_pq_runtime_memory_budget, native_pq_runtime_memory_adjustment, native_pq_runtime_artifacts_written, native_pq_runtime_artifacts_consumed, native_pq_runtime_official_pq_used, native_pq_runtime_bridge, and backend_build_fallback_reason. The native_pq_runtime_* fields are populated when vector_diskann_pq_runtime is native_auto or native_strict. Strict mode accepts a build only after file layout/checksum, ordinal/doc-id mapping, load, and search-result-shape validation succeeds, with written and consumed artifacts, official-PQ usage of 0, bridge native_pq_graph_cache, and native_pq_runtime_artifact_validation=ok. An approximate self-hit miss may coexist with that ok status. In that case native_pq_runtime_validation_failed=1, and the diagnostic fields record the failed sample doc-id, best returned doc-id, result count, approximate and exact best-result distances, self PQ reconstruction distance, and the recomputed pivots/compressed checksums. Scheduler diagnostics include scheduler_path, scheduler_segment_count, scheduler_task_count, scheduler_concurrent_tasks, scheduler_cpu_budget, scheduler_effective_build_threads, scheduler_effective_blas_threads, scheduler_candidates_per_segment, scheduler_effective_segment_tasks, scheduler_segment_memory_estimate, scheduler_segment_memory_budget, and scheduler_segment_parallel_reason. The segment memory fields show whether a nonzero vector_diskann_build_memory_size reduced outer segment-build concurrency. After a segmented search, scheduler_search_fanout_segments, scheduler_search_fanout_threads, scheduler_search_worker_budget, scheduler_search_active_requests, scheduler_search_work_items, scheduler_search_global_top_k, scheduler_search_per_segment_top_k, scheduler_search_result_budget, scheduler_search_candidate_count, scheduler_search_query_count, scheduler_search_segment_min_entries, scheduler_search_segment_max_entries, scheduler_search_segment_total_entries, scheduler_search_diskann_search_list, scheduler_search_diskann_beamwidth, scheduler_search_effective_complexity, scheduler_search_profile, scheduler_search_profile_reason, scheduler_search_total_candidate_rows, scheduler_search_advice, scheduler_search_advice_reason, scheduler_search_suggested_complexity, and scheduler_search_suggested_segment_target_size expose the last search fan-out and budget actually used. For segmented HNSW, scheduler_search_worker_budget is the process-wide worker limit, scheduler_search_active_requests is the active-request snapshot when the request entered the shared pool, and scheduler_search_work_items is the segment count for one query or segment_count * query_count for a batch. scheduler_search_global_top_k is the final SQL topK requested by the query, scheduler_search_query_count is the batch query count, and scheduler_search_total_candidate_rows is candidate_count * query_count. For DiskANN, scheduler_search_per_segment_top_k is the returned-candidate window for each segment, scheduler_search_candidate_count is the across-segment returned-candidate total per query, scheduler_search_diskann_search_list/scheduler_search_effective_complexity show the effective DiskANN search list, scheduler_search_diskann_beamwidth shows beamwidth, and the profile fields show whether the value came from manual, fast, balanced, high_recall, or a preserved higher explicit value. The advice fields classify common low-recall risks, including too-small fan-out candidate windows, batch candidate-budget truncation, and large segments that should use smaller segment targets or a high-recall profile. These candidate windows are intentionally kept smaller than diskann_search_complexity when possible, because diskann_search_complexity is the search list, not the returned-candidate count; multi-segment search divides search_complexity - 1 by the fan-out count before the final global topK merge. DiskANN-specific segment diagnostics include diskann_segment_pq_chunks, diskann_segment_cache_nodes, diskann_segment_build_wall_ms, diskann_segment_build_sum_ms, and diskann_segment_fallback_reason; non-DiskANN providers report 0 for DiskANN numeric fields and not_applicable for the fallback reason. After successful DiskANN native build/rebuild, external_manifest_present is 0 because the active serving index comes from the native store or committed-entry fallback.

      Additional exact BACKEND_HEALTH fields:

      Field suffixMeaning
      backend_typeProvider name used by the current serving backend.
      loaded1 when the serving runtime is loaded.
      writable1 when the loaded backend currently accepts mutations.
      recover_fallback_countNumber of recoveries that used a supported fallback path.
      last_recover_fallback_tsUnix timestamp of the latest recovery fallback, or 0 when none occurred.
      external_manifest_generationGeneration published by the current external sidecar manifest.
      consistency_modeIndex consistency mode: transactional or standalone.
      truth_store_enabled1 when the backend uses the InnoDB truth-store as its authoritative input.
      build_sourceInput source used to install the latest serving backend; values match INDEX_STATE.build_source.
      index_identityDurable index identity bound to the current backend runtime.
      truth_generationCurrent authoritative-input generation.
      config_generationCurrent persisted configuration generation.
      artifact_generationCurrent recoverable backend-artifact generation.
      runtime_generationCurrent serving-runtime generation, used to determine whether runtime has caught up with truth.
      backend_build_concurrent_tasksMaximum outer build tasks that ran concurrently during the latest build.
      backend_build_effective_threadsWorkers actually granted to one provider build task.
      backend_build_effective_blas_threadsBLAS/OpenMP threads actually used by one provider build task.
      backend_build_raw_reader_threadsRaw-reader threads used by the latest build.
      backend_build_pq_train_threadsThreads used by the latest PQ training stage.
      backend_build_pq_compress_threadsThreads used by the latest PQ compression/encoding stage.
      backend_build_single_index1 when the latest build used single-index semantics rather than outer multi-segment builds.
      diskann_effective_disk_pq_dimsDisk-PQ dimensions actually written by the latest DiskANN build; emitted only when nonzero.
      backend_build_reader_count_msMilliseconds spent by the Faiss scheduler counting training/build input rows.
      backend_build_training_copy_msMilliseconds spent by the Faiss scheduler copying training samples.
      backend_build_train_msMilliseconds spent by the Faiss scheduler training the index.
      backend_build_add_msMilliseconds spent by the Faiss scheduler adding vectors to the trained index.
      backend_build_persist_msMilliseconds spent by the Faiss scheduler persisting the snapshot.
      backend_build_training_rowsVectors actually used for Faiss training.
      backend_build_reader_passesInput-reader passes used for count, training, and add.
      scheduler_raw_reader_threadsThreads assigned by the scheduler to raw input reading.
      scheduler_pq_train_threadsThreads assigned by the scheduler to PQ training.
      scheduler_pq_compress_threadsThreads assigned by the scheduler to PQ compression/encoding.
      scheduler_single_index_build1 when the scheduler selected single-index build.
      diskann_segment_effective_disk_pq_dimsDisk-PQ dimensions used by the latest DiskANN segment build; omitted for non-DiskANN providers and uncompressed layouts.
      native_pq_runtime_artifact_validationNative PQ artifact validation result, such as ok, or the validation failure reason.
      native_pq_runtime_train_rowsRows used by the latest Native PQ training stage.
      native_pq_runtime_compressed_rowsRows encoded into Native PQ compressed codes.

      Native PQ timing fields split the strict build into input read (native_pq_runtime_raw_reader_ms), training (native_pq_runtime_train_ms), block encoding (native_pq_runtime_encode_ms), artifact validation (native_pq_runtime_artifact_validation_ms), bridge consumption (native_pq_runtime_bridge_ms), graph construction (native_pq_runtime_graph_ms), and serving-cache preparation (native_pq_runtime_cache_ms). The row counters report the actual training samples and compressed vectors. A successful strict build reports native_pq_runtime_artifact_validation=ok after file layout/checksum, ordinal/doc-id mapping, load, and search-result-shape validation. native_pq_runtime_validation_failed is 1 when a fixed sample misses itself in the approximate-search topK or the search returns an invalid result count. The former is diagnostic only; the latter also sets the hard-validation status. native_pq_runtime_validation_failed_doc_id identifies the first affected sample, native_pq_runtime_validation_best_doc_id and native_pq_runtime_validation_result_count describe the returned candidate set, and native_pq_runtime_validation_best_search_distance and native_pq_runtime_validation_best_exact_distance compare the backend and exact distances. native_pq_runtime_validation_self_pq_distance reports the L2 distance to the reconstructed PQ vector. The native_pq_runtime_validation_pivots_checksum and native_pq_runtime_validation_compressed_checksum fields contain recomputed artifact checksums, or unavailable when the complete bridge artifact set cannot be read. These diagnostics do not override an otherwise successful hard-validation status.

      After the latest build/rebuild completes, BACKEND_HEALTH also reports the admission envelope. backend_build_resource_source is cgroup_v2, cgroup_v1, or host. backend_build_resource_configured_memory and backend_build_resource_memory_reserve are the root configuration and safety reserve. backend_build_resource_memory_limit, backend_build_resource_memory_current, backend_build_resource_process_rss, and backend_build_resource_memory_headroom form the probe snapshot. backend_build_resource_effective_memory and backend_build_resource_effective_cpu_slots are the lease granted to that build. backend_build_resource_reserved_memory, backend_build_resource_reserved_cpu_slots, backend_build_resource_active_builds, and backend_build_resource_waiting_builds are the process-wide concurrency state when diagnostics were recorded.

      SYNC_PIPELINE fields:

      Field suffix Meaning
      pending_apply_count Non-negative difference between entry_count and committed_entry_count; it represents the backlog between the current serving backend and the authoritative source. During bulk-load it usually equals the number of imported entries not yet visible through the backend.
      apply_latency_ms Time spent by the latest apply operation.
      rebuild_progress Rebuild state indicator: 100 means ready, 50 means rebuilding, and other states report 0.
      recover_progress Recovery state indicator: 100 means ready, 50 means recovering, and other states report 0.

      Lifecycle state values:

      Value Meaning
      ready The index is readable and writable, and the latest create, rebuild, recover, or commit has completed.
      creating Table-level CREATE VECTOR INDEX metadata has been persisted but backfill has not started; restart recovery removes this incomplete DDL state.
      backfilling Table-level CREATE VECTOR INDEX is scanning the base table and building the initial committed/serving snapshot; queries fail, and restart recovery removes this incomplete DDL state.
      bulk_loading The index is in bulk-load mode. Commits update only truth rows; queries fail until VEC_INDEX_BULK_BUILD() or VEC_INDEX_REBUILD() succeeds.
      rebuilding The serving index is being rebuilt. The current synchronous rebuild API builds a shadow backend outside the lock, so this is usually visible only through explicit diagnostic state.
      recovering The index is recovering from persistent state or committed entries. The current synchronous recover API builds a shadow backend outside the lock, so this is usually visible only through explicit diagnostic state.
      failed The latest lifecycle operation for this index failed; inspect last_error_code and last_error_ts.

      Per-index lifecycle_state=failed is different from a global registry fail-stop. If rollback cannot fully restore runtime state, pending state, or persisted artifacts, the registry enters global fail-stop. Subsequent searches and administrative operations that require vector metadata fail instead of serving a possibly divergent state. Vector_runtime_state_rollback_failures or Vector_persist_artifact_rollback_failures increases in this condition; these counters are alerts and do not clear fail-stop. Stop vector writes and restart the instance so startup recovery reloads the last provably complete durable state.

      Error codes:

      Error code Meaning
      0No error.
      1001Uncommitted changes exist, so the current operation cannot safely execute.
      1002Backend creation failed.
      1003Backend recovery failed.
      1004Committed-entry replay failed.
      1005The backend does not support mutation.
      1006Backend apply failed.

      7.4 Actual status query output examples

      Excerpt of actual output for independent named index idx_fn. Complete output also contains the pipeline, DiskANN tuning, and publication-generation fields listed above:

      SHOW VECTOR STATUS LIKE 'idx_fn.%';

| INDEX_STATE    | idx_fn.lifecycle_state              | ready     |
| INDEX_STATE    | idx_fn.entry_count                  | 1         |
| INDEX_STATE    | idx_fn.committed_entry_count        | 1         |

| BACKEND_HEALTH | idx_fn.loaded                       | 1         |

      7.5 Other status entrypoints

      • SHOW GLOBAL STATUS LIKE 'Vector_%'
      • SHOW ENGINE INNODB STATUS

      All variables with the Vector_ prefix in SHOW GLOBAL STATUS LIKE 'Vector_%' are vector-related status. They are roughly grouped by name:

      Status prefix Meaning
      Vector_index_*Vector index create/drop request counters.
      Vector_metadata_*Vector index metadata load and persist failure counters.
      Vector_committed_*Committed-entry snapshot row count, committed vector cache bytes, and read/write failure counters.
      Vector_manifest_*Vector manifest version, checkpoint, and read/write failure counters.
      Vector_change_log_*Vector change-log load, replay, and persist failure counters.
      Vector_backend_*Backend recovery fallback, provider/mode/manifest, and related backend state.
      Vector_build_resource_*Process-wide vector-build probe source, memory/CPU root envelope, current reservations, active/waiting builds, and the latest admission result.
      Vector_rebuild_*, Vector_recover_*Rebuild and recovery request counters.
      Vector_search_*Search requests, failures, returned results, and mapped MVCC candidate expansion counters.
      Vector_stage_*, Vector_txn_*, Vector_pending_txn_changes, Vector_pending_vector_memory_bytesExplicit transaction buffer, pending payload memory, and commit/rollback state.
      Vector_runtime_state_*, Vector_persist_artifact_*Runtime-state and persisted-artifact rollback state.
      Vector_truth_store_*Truth-store backend, transaction capability, and full/incremental persist and compact counters.
      Vector_apply_latency_msTime spent by the latest vector change apply.
      Vector_registered_indexesNumber of currently registered vector indexes.
