      In SHOW ENGINE INNODB STATUS, only the VECTOR OBSERVABILITY subsection is vector-related. Transaction, lock, buffer pool, and other sections in the same output remain native InnoDB status. This subsection keeps only the truth-store, manifest, change-log, artifact rollback, and other persistence/engine-boundary summaries. Use SHOW GLOBAL STATUS LIKE 'Vector_%' or SHOW VECTOR STATUS for SQL registry state such as index lifecycle, provider/mode, query, rebuild, and explicit transaction state. Actual output example:

      mysql> SHOW ENGINE INNODB STATUS;
...
--------------------
VECTOR OBSERVABILITY
--------------------
vector metadata: load_fail=0, persist_fail=0
vector truth store: backend=mysql, transactional=1
vector truth-store persist: requests=2, failures=0
vector committed snapshot: rows=1, load_fail=0, persist_fail=0
vector manifest: version=3, metadata_ckpt=1, committed_ckpt=1, change_log_ckpt=1, load_fail=0, persist_fail=0
vector change-log: load_fail=0, replay_fail=0, persist_fail=0, backend_recover_fallback=0
vector artifact rollback: persist_artifact=0, persist_artifact_fail=0
...

      Recommendations:

      • Use SHOW GLOBAL STATUS LIKE 'Vector_%' for lightweight global monitoring.
      • Use SHOW VECTOR STATUS FOR INDEX ... for precise per-index diagnostics.
      • Use SHOW ENGINE INNODB STATUS when diagnosing truth-store / manifest / change-log persistence summaries.
      • SHOW GLOBAL STATUS is global-only; for per-index backlog, standalone source state, and build source, use SHOW VECTOR STATUS or VEC_INDEX_INFO().

      8. Usage recommendations

      8.1 General guidance

      • Use VEC_FROMTEXT(...) consistently for text vector input to avoid client-side binary encoding differences.
      • For production table-column paths, prefer table-level DDL with normal DML because synchronization behavior is more direct.
      • Before tuning HNSW / Faiss / DiskANN, confirm the current index mode/provider.
      • When consuming machine-readable results, treat VEC_INDEX_INFO() / VEC_INDEX_SEARCH*() as JSON text.
      • After changing index parameters in batches, explicitly run VEC_INDEX_REBUILD(...) when build-time settings must take effect immediately.
      • Set vector_pending_cache_size to 0 to remove the MySQL-layer pending payload limit. For a nonzero limit, use at least 1M; very small values cause frequent spill and reduce the usefulness of the uncommitted vector cache.
      • The default vector_build_memory_size=0 limits all concurrent builds from cgroup/host headroom and keeps vector_build_memory_reserve_size outside that budget. Nonzero memory requests are never silently clipped: a request above the current effective root budget fails immediately, while a satisfiable request waits only when another build temporarily holds the capacity. In containers, inspect Vector_build_resource_* to confirm the probe source and granted lease. Set an explicit root ceiling only when a fixed limit is required; do not configure every backend as though it owned all host memory.
      • While a query or administrative statement waits for an index or catalog publication reservation, it polls for statement KILL and server shutdown. Cancellation during this waiting phase returns an error and releases waiter accounting so unrelated index operations are not kept blocked. This guarantee covers only the wait before reservation acquisition; it does not imply that a call already executing inside a third-party backend can be interrupted asynchronously.

      8.2 Performance parameter guidance

      • Before integrating an external benchmark, run a small dataset first to confirm table creation, index creation, writes, queries, and parameter mapping, then switch to a large dataset.
      • For large imports, pin the build pipeline settings first so different test rounds do not accidentally switch paths at scale boundaries: 
        SET GLOBAL vector_build_pipeline_mode = 'auto';
SET GLOBAL vector_build_pipeline_min_rows = 1048576;
SET GLOBAL vector_build_pipeline_min_size = 4294967296;
SET GLOBAL vector_build_segment_max_rows = 4294967295;
SET GLOBAL vector_build_segment_target_size = 2147483648;
SET GLOBAL vector_build_pipeline_max_tasks = 1;

        For path diagnosis, temporarily set vector_build_pipeline_mode to direct or segmented. Do not use very small segments as a default performance setting unless the goal is explicitly to trade higher build cost for higher recall. The default recommendation is a 2GiB segment target with vector_build_segment_max_rows kept as the safety cap; lower the row cap explicitly only when matching another system's segment layout or reproducing recall behavior.

        DiskANN standalone segmented serving recall depends on both segment size and search_complexity. Smaller segments increase build invocations and query fan-out, but each per-segment candidate window can cover useful neighbors more easily. Larger segments usually improve QPS, but may require a larger search_complexity to reach the same recall. If recall is the target, scan segment target and search complexity together. If throughput is the target, start with larger segments and then increase search complexity gradually.

        vector_diskann_search_profile and VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_COMPLEXITY() are user-tunable query parameters, but the profile is not a universal recall switch. If VEC_INDEX_INFO() or SHOW VECTOR STATUS reports fanout_candidate_window_too_small, continue scanning the higher scheduler_search_suggested_complexity values, such as 1600/2400/3200, and tune segment target together with search complexity; do not treat high_recall alone as proof that the target recall has been reached.

      • For large DiskANN builds and queries, pin these core parameters first:
        • vector_diskann_build_mode='offline' uses the DiskANN offline build path.
        • vector_diskann_max_degree=56 and vector_diskann_build_complexity=100 are a high-recall graph starting point.
        • vector_diskann_build_threads=32 should match the test machine's available build parallelism.
        • vector_diskann_build_blas_threads=1 avoids multiplying outer DiskANN OpenMP workers by inner PQ or numeric-library workers during offline build. Increase it only for explicit numeric-library parallelism experiments.
        • vector_diskann_offline_search_threads=64 aligns the searcher-thread cap used while loading a DiskANN offline index. Batch-query parallelism is controlled by vector_diskann_search_threads or vector_batch_search_threads.
        • vector_diskann_search_beamwidth=16 is a conservative capped beam width; the maximum is 128, and this value should not be scaled with search_complexity.
        • vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_size=0 derives the PQ code budget from vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_ratio; the default ratio is 0.125.
        • A 1024D float vector is 4096 raw bytes/vector; the default 0.125 ratio derives an approximately 512 bytes/vector PQ-code budget. Lower values usually reduce index size and build memory, but may reduce recall.
        • vector_diskann_disk_pq_dims=0 uses recall-first automatic layout selection for DiskANN disk nodes. MySQL-Vector keeps raw vectors when a full-vector node record fits one 4KB sector; when high dimensionality or a larger max_degree would exceed the sector, it uses the largest sector-safe disk-PQ dimension capped by the DiskANN C++ loader chunk limit of 512, instead of creating an unsearchable or unloadable layout. Set a nonzero value only when you need an explicit compression target.
        • vector_diskann_cache_nodes is a node count, not a byte budget. A nonzero value takes precedence, is capped by the index row count, and synchronously builds a BFS static cache while loading the DiskANN offline index.
        • When vector_diskann_cache_nodes=0, MySQL-Vector uses vector_diskann_search_cache_size as the cache byte budget. If that value is also 0, it derives the budget from vector_diskann_search_cache_ratio * raw vector bytes. The final node count uses the DiskANN cached-node footprint and a 1.2 expansion factor, so the default ratio 0.1 is not a fixed 10% row cache.

        Large DiskANN benchmarks must record both the build memory budget and pipeline concurrency. When tuning these values, also inspect scheduler_segment_memory_budget, scheduler_segment_memory_estimate, scheduler_segment_parallel_reason, actual store count, CPU/RSS/I/O, and stack samples.

        SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_build_mode = 'offline';
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_max_degree = 56;
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_build_complexity = 100;
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_build_threads = 32;
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_build_blas_threads = 1;
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_offline_search_threads = 64;
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_search_complexity = 100;
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_search_beamwidth = 16;
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_size = 0;
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_ratio = 0.125;
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_search_cache_size = 0;
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_search_cache_ratio = 0.1;
SET GLOBAL vector_diskann_cache_nodes = 10000;

SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_COMPLEXITY('idx_diskann', 100);
SELECT VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_BEAMWIDTH('idx_diskann', 16);

        VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_BUILD_PARAMS(), VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_BUILD_MODE(), and VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_PQ_CODE_BUDGET_SIZE() are build-time configuration. For benchmarks, set them through global variables before creating the index and loading data. If you change them on an existing index, rebuild explicitly; do not use them as per-query tuning functions.

      8.3 High-throughput import

      • For high-dimensional large-batch imports, prefer the standalone named-index fast path: create the index with VEC_INDEX_CREATE(), load file-based data with LOAD VECTOR DATA ... FORMAT FBIN REBUILD so the server writes standalone raw segments and rebuilds once, then query with VEC_INDEX_SEARCH().
      • When the client already has binary batch payloads, use VEC_INDEX_UPSERT_BATCH() followed by VEC_INDEX_REBUILD().
      • Use row-by-row VEC_INDEX_UPSERT() only for manual diagnostics or very small datasets.
      • Large imports should pass binary float32 vector payloads directly from the client. VEC_FROMTEXT('[...]') is better for SQL examples, small smoke tests, and manual diagnostics.

      8.4 Query tuning

      • For query load tests or online batch retrieval, prefer VEC_INDEX_SEARCH_BATCH() instead of issuing many client-side VEC_INDEX_SEARCH() calls in a loop. The batch function reduces SQL round trips and registry/service/backend dispatch overhead. One batch supports up to 1024 queries, and query_count * top_k must not exceed 65536.
      • When tuning Faiss IVF / IVFPQ, scan nprobe first. If nlist, pq_m, and pq_bits stay unchanged, changing only nprobe is query-time tuning and does not rebuild the index. Changing nlist, pq_m, or pq_bits is build-time tuning and requires regenerating the serving index.
      • When tuning DiskANN search, first scan the recall / latency curve with VEC_INDEX_SET_DISKANN_SEARCH_COMPLEXITY(). For batch-query load tests, use vector_diskann_search_threads or the global vector_batch_search_threads to tune read-only workers inside one batch. This affects batch query only and does not change single-query semantics.
      • When diagnosing segmented DiskANN search, first inspect the actual fan-out and candidate budget with VEC_INDEX_INFO() or SHOW VECTOR STATUS: scheduler_search_fanout_segments, scheduler_search_per_segment_top_k, scheduler_search_candidate_count, scheduler_search_diskann_search_list, scheduler_search_diskann_beamwidth, scheduler_search_effective_complexity, scheduler_search_profile, scheduler_search_profile_reason, and scheduler_search_total_candidate_rows, scheduler_search_advice, scheduler_search_advice_reason, scheduler_search_suggested_complexity, and scheduler_search_suggested_segment_target_size. These fields confirm whether the query really used the expected search list, profile, beamwidth, segment count, and batch candidate window, and they give the next tuning direction when low recall is likely.
      • DiskANN offline/compressed search returns backend candidates first, then MySQL-Vector reranks the candidates with the exact vectors stored by the index before returning the final top-K. For batch-query recall tuning, vector_diskann_exact_rerank_candidates controls the preferred per-query candidate window before exact rerank; 0 derives it automatically. A larger candidate window usually improves compressed-index recall, but it increases exact-vector reads and rerank cost. vector_search_batch_result_count is only the total result-slot cap for one batch and must be large enough for query_count * candidate_top_k, otherwise batch search truncates the candidate window.
      • hnswlib and Faiss batch query can use vector_hnsw_search_threads and vector_faiss_search_threads to override the global worker count. These settings do not improve single-query latency when the workload uses only VEC_INDEX_SEARCH().
      • Segmented HNSW places single-query segment fan-out work, or batch segment x query work, in one process-wide worker pool so SQL concurrency, segment count, and batch workers do not multiply the thread count. Inspect scheduler_search_worker_budget, scheduler_search_active_requests, and scheduler_search_work_items to confirm the effective budget and flattened work size.
      • The current public ANN API has no native filter parameter. To validate filter semantics, write it as an exact query such as WHERE ... ORDER BY VEC_DISTANCE(...) LIMIT k. Do not describe over-fetch or post-filter results as native ANN filtering.

      8.5 Load-test troubleshooting

      • Long-running load tests should use an isolated mysqld instance and reserve enough innodb_redo_log_capacity, innodb_buffer_pool_size, and backend build threads, so redo recycling, buffer pool churn, or low parallelism is not mistaken for a vector-index bottleneck.
      • If a benchmark runner separates load, optimize, and search into different processes, later processes should first call VEC_INDEX_INFO(index_name) to attach to existing index state instead of blindly repeating VEC_INDEX_CREATE(...).
      • If VEC_INDEX_INFO() already reports the target search_ef, repeated VEC_INDEX_SET_SEARCH_EF(...) is usually unnecessary; reissuing it only updates metadata/manifest.
      • When a load test becomes slow or interrupted, first distinguish whether the client import path or the server index build path is slow:
        • Use SHOW PROCESSLIST to see the SQL currently running in each session.
        • Use SHOW GLOBAL STATUS LIKE 'Vector_%' to inspect write, commit, persist, and search counters.
        • Use SHOW ENGINE INNODB STATUS to inspect truth-store, manifest, and change-log persistence summaries.
      • When validating non-default DiskANN parameters, record the corresponding MySQL system variables in the test configuration:
        • vector_diskann_max_degree
        • vector_diskann_build_complexity
        • vector_diskann_search_complexity
        • vector_diskann_pq_code_budget_size

      9.3 Build configuration

      The measured CMakeCache maps to the command below; machine-specific paths use portable variables. FAISS and DiskANN are statically linked, while HNSWLIB uses its source header-only integration. ldd mysqld contains no dynamic FAISS or DiskANN dependency. MYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_OPT_LEVEL was not set, so the build used the Native SIMD default dispatch policy.

      cmake -S "${MYSQL_SOURCE_DIR}" -B "${BUILD_DIR}" -G Ninja \
  -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release \
  -DWITH_DEBUG=0 \
  -DWITH_ROUTER=OFF \
  -DWITH_UNIT_TESTS=ON \
  -DWITH_NDB=OFF \
  -DWITH_NDBCLUSTER_STORAGE_ENGINE=OFF \
  -DWITH_LOCK_ORDER=ON \
  -DDOWNLOAD_BOOST=1 \
  -DWITH_BOOST="${BOOST_ROOT}" \
  -DWITH_SSL=system \
  -DWITH_ZLIB=bundled \
  -DWITH_LZ4=bundled \
  -DWITH_ZSTD=bundled \
  -DWITH_PROTOBUF=bundled \
  -DWITH_ICU=bundled \
  -DHAVE_VECTOR_INDEX=ON \
  -DMYSQL_VECTOR_ENABLE_NATIVE_SIMD=ON \
  -DMYSQL_VECTOR_HNSW=ON \
  -DMYSQL_VECTOR_HNSWLIB_ROOT="${HNSWLIB_ROOT}" \
  -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS=ON \
  -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_ROOT="${FAISS_ROOT}" \
  -DMYSQL_VECTOR_FAISS_STATIC=ON \
  -DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN=ON \
  -DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_ROOT="${DISKANN_ROOT}" \
  -DMYSQL_VECTOR_DISKANN_STATIC=ON

cmake --build "${BUILD_DIR}" --parallel 32

      9.7 Scope and limitations

      • These results cover only the high-performance Standalone raw-FBIN path with the binlog disabled; they do not represent table-bound Transactional mode.
      • HNSW/FAISS also use innodb_flush_log_at_trx_commit=2 and sync_binlog=0; the table is not a full-durability performance guarantee.
      • The results apply to the listed hardware, compiler, backend versions, and parameters. They document current MySQL-Vector behavior; this chapter does not compare products.
